```
1	---
2	name: Porting epic and workflow
3	description: SPI-238 epic for porting dev_sprint to dev — 13 tickets, 5-phase agent workflow, parallel lanes, dependency order
4	type: project
5	---
6	
7	Epic SPI-238 tracks porting all capability slices from dev_sprint to dev in dependency order.
8	
9	**Why:** dev_sprint has ~500 files of feature work (FS1-FS12, storage, validation, QC, LLM routing, API) that needs to land on dev as reviewable, tested PRs — not a raw merge.
10	
11	**How to apply:**
12	- 13 sub-tickets (SPI-225 through SPI-237), each one port slice
13	- SPI-225 (storage) and SPI-226 (prompt/LLM) already landed
14	- SPI-227 (document storage) in progress
15	- 4 parallel lanes possible; see epic description for dependency graph
16	- Every ticket follows a 5-phase workflow: Research → Harmonize → Ask questions (BLOCKING) → Implement → Open PR
17	- Phase 2 harmonization findings go as a comment on the Linear ticket for discussion
18	- Phase 3 is a hard gate — no implementation without explicit user approval
19	- PRs reference the SPI ticket number in the title
20	- Don't move to dependent tickets until blocking PRs are merged on dev
21	- No need to pin dev_sprint commit — use whatever is current at time of work
22	- Tickets with dedicated test plans (FS5, FS6, FS7) must follow them; others must propose test approach for approval
23	- Status vocabulary from AGENTS.md: pending, in_progress, implemented (code exists but not verified), verified (all gates passed)
24	
```

Excellent. I've now read all five design documents from the dev_sprint branch. Let me provide you with a comprehensive summary focused on Phase 1 research for your SPI-228 live graph gate repair port.

---

## PHASE 1 RESEARCH: SPI-228 LIVE GRAPH GATE REPAIR (P-E2E-GRAPH-01)

### DOCUMENT 1: top-level-process-and-analytical-tests.md

**P-E2E-GRAPH-01 Gate Definition (from 2026-05-08 clarification):**

P-E2E-GRAPH-01 is the **blocking S8 process gate** for complete-run compliant graph surfaces. This is a live port tick requirement—deterministic fixtures cannot earn the port tick; only provider-backed complete DB-backed runs qualify.

**Canonical Terms:**
- Process gates are deterministic and mechanical; must pass before dependent stages proceed
- Analytical gates measure quality (training-set improvability) and become blocking once labels/rubrics/thresholds/splits freeze
- FS10 owns the validation-data lifecycle: source candidates, packages, labels, rubrics, splits, exclusions, freeze approvals
- FS11 owns QC/gating decisions and defensibility measurement families: Analytical, Research, Driver, Indicator, Graph

**Gate Details for P-E2E-GRAPH-01:**

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | End-to-end graph return |
| **Earliest runnable** | S8 (after FS7 lands) |
| **Cumulative rerun** | S8 after any core feature slice, graph-persistence, graph-API, inference, summary, or report change |
| **Harness dependency** | `FS10-H8`; `FS11-H8` |
| **Authority contract** | Live complete-run DB state: `graphs`, `nodes`, `edges`, subtype rows, run refs, returned graph payloads |
| **Pass condition** | A live complete DB-backed run succeeds, returns graph data, persists graph rows, and `validate_graph_entities` reports no issues |
| **Evidence requirement** | **Live provider-backed complete-run evidence required; deterministic fixtures only prove sample-runnable structure** |
| **Blocking** | Yes—core acceptance path |

**Key Validation Rule:**
Live complete-run evidence is required for the port tick. The gate is marked `[x]` only when:
1. A production-lock run with live provider succeeds (not `sample_runnable`)
2. Returned graph payloads validate cleanly
3. Persisted graph rows (per question-run ID) validate cleanly
4. Full graph and driver-indicator subgraph validation issue counts = 0

---

### DOCUMENT 2: fs-capability-test-coverage.md

**P-E2E-GRAPH-01 Capability Row:**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **FS** | E2E |
| **Capability** | Complete live DB-backed run returning and persisting a compliant graph |
| **Process gate** | `P-E2E-GRAPH-01` |
| **Analytical gates** | `A-E2E-01`; `A-E2E-02` |
| **Validation package** | `VD-GRAPH-CONSTRUCTION`, `VD-RESOLVED-FORECAST-REPLAY` |
| **Coverage status** | Covered |
| **Blocking status** | Blocking |
| **Notes** | Port go/no-go for graph/runtime requires live evidence; controlled fixtures support sample-runnable structure only |

**Coverage Matrix Semantics:**

Analytical coverage means the capability is **improvable on its associated frozen training split through the owning feature set's tuning architecture**. Held-out eval tracks generalization and promotion review, but the capability row is analytically covered when FS10 can prove train-split improvement.

For complete-run graph surface: **covered only when top-level process gate P-E2E-GRAPH-01 is named and evaluated. Its port tick requires a live complete DB-backed run that returns graph data, persists graph rows, and passes canonical graph-entity validation.**

---

### DOCUMENT 3: graph-entity-definitions.md

**Canonical Terms (Graph Semantics):**

| Term | Meaning | Verification Basis |
|---|---|---|
| **indicator** | Concrete, present-tense fact, source-verifiable, materially informative | 5 required elements: subject, quantified state, source, verification date, material relevance |
| **driver** | Judgment that an event/condition changes probability of option resolving true | 3 linked clauses: event/condition, mechanism, impact |
| **target** | Formally defined future outcome resolved at specified time | 4 elements: outcome definition, resolution time, resolution source, exclusivity structure |
| **information** | Source-attributed information object from which indicators/drivers are derived/verified | 4 elements: content reference, source/attribution, access reference, temporal context |
| **CONDITIONAL edge** | Probabilistic relationship; source node can meaningfully change target node probability | Valid endpoints: `indicator->driver`, `indicator->target`, `driver->driver`, `driver->target` |
| **REFERENCE edge** | Non-causal provenance/traceability only; two subtypes: `source` and `evidence` | Valid endpoints: `information->indicator`, `information->driver` (NOT `information->target` in active graphs) |

**Validation Rules (Critical for P-E2E-GRAPH-01):**

1. **Node types:** only `target`, `driver`, `indicator`, `information` are canonical
   - `option`, `evidence` are legacy; validator aliases them for repair; DB maps to canonical types
   - `question` nodes are not canonical graph entities

2. **Edge semantics:**
   - `CONDITIONAL` edges encode probabilistic influence
   - `REFERENCE` edges are non-causal, provenance-only
   - `driver -> target REFERENCE` is **invalid** in active forecast graphs
   - `information -> target REFERENCE` is **invalid** in active forecast graphs

3. **Exclusivity rule:** Targets must be mutually exclusive and exhaustive in a graph

4. **Fanout rule:** If there is one `driver -> target` edge, there must be edges from that driver to all other targets (complete effect over target space)

**On dev_sprint (added):**
- `run_research_driver_for_question` gains `run_id`, `model_set`, `llm_profile`, `run_config_id`, `run_config_snapshot`

- **`_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()`** -- filters reduced drivers through the graph delta's admitted driver list, only keeping drivers that were actually admitted to the graph and have literature sources. This is a key graph gate repair function.
- **`_has_indicator_literature_sources()`** -- checks whether a driver has PDF refs or source contexts with document identifiers. This filters out drivers that have no literature basis before running indicator extraction.
- Step 4 uses `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers(drivers_payload)` instead of raw `reduced_drivers` list
- Thread pool uses `submit_with_context()` for context propagation
- `_sync_fs2_document_blob_authority()` -- registers graph document blobs for FS2 evidence
- `_sync_fs2_runtime_authority()` -- syncs baseline, current resolution, research defensibility evidence, and runs P-FS2-01 process-authority gate
- `_compute_graph_brier_metrics()` / `_resolved_question_truth()` -- computes Brier scores when answer is resolved
- `extraction_debug` result includes `"graph_delta"` key
- `upsert_graph` uses absolute graph counts instead of deltas; adds Brier fields
- `upsert_summary_entry` called after graph save
- `save_run_steps` includes per-step LLM cost breakdowns (calls, tokens, USD, by_model)
- `_build_version_stamp()` included in metrics writes
- `prompt_storage_config` / `prompt_trace_run_id` threaded to extraction stage
- `sources_payload` explicitly copied via `dict()`
- Failed-retrieval path now persists partial run_steps and update_run_metrics
- `historical_data_summary` captured and threaded to FS2 runtime authority sync
- Result payload gains `"document_blob_authority"`, `"fs2_runtime_authority"`, `"historical_data_summary"`

**SPI-228 scope changes needed (graph validation/indicator paths, the core of the ticket):**

The two highest-priority changes for "live graph gate repair":
1. **`_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()`** and **`_has_indicator_literature_sources()`** -- these functions gate which drivers enter indicator-literature processing, ensuring only graph-admitted drivers with actual literature sources are processed. This is the literal "graph gate repair" referenced in the ticket title.
2. **Step 4 change**: replacing `drivers_payload.get("reduced_drivers")` with `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers(drivers_payload)` -- this is the one-line behavioral fix that ensures indicator literature only runs on graph-admitted drivers.

Supporting changes that are likely in-scope:
- `extraction_debug` including `"graph_delta"` key (needed for `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers` to work)
- `_compute_graph_brier_metrics()` and `_resolved_question_truth()` for graph-level quality tracking
- `upsert_graph` using absolute counts and adding Brier fields
- `upsert_summary_entry` for graph summary persistence

Changes that are likely cross-cutting / shared with other SPI tickets:
- `run_id` / `model_set` / `run_config_id` / `config_snapshot` threading (SPI-214 / run-config infrastructure)
- `_load_qc_steps_payload` DB-first QC reading
- `_sync_fs2_document_blob_authority` / `_sync_fs2_runtime_authority` (FS2 evidence chain)
- `submit_with_context` (concurrency context propagation)
- Per-step LLM cost breakdowns in `save_run_steps`
- `_build_version_stamp` in metrics
- Logger warnings replacing bare `pass`
- Failed-retrieval partial metrics persistence

### Summary of SPI-228 Core Scope (graph gate repair)

The ticket's primary behavioral change is in `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py`:
- Two new functions `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()` and `_has_indicator_literature_sources()` that filter reduced drivers through the graph delta before running indicator-literature extraction
- The Step 4 entry point change from raw `reduced_drivers` to the filtered list
- The `"graph_delta"` key addition in extraction debug output (prerequisite data)

Supporting changes in `config/llm_tasks.toml`:
- `FS3.indicator_extraction` tier change (`large` to `medium`)
- Two new entries: `FS3.indicator_deduplication_prompt`, `FS3.driver_indicator_network_tuning`

The CLI files (`commands.py`, `parser.py`) have significant cross-cutting changes (run-config system, model-set, provider preflight, FS10/FS12 subcommands) that go well beyond graph gate repair. The graph-specific subset is the `model_overrides` to `model_set` transition and `max_parallel_questions` addition, but these feel like prerequisites or co-travelers rather than the core SPI-228 scope.

## Phase 1 Research: SPI-228 Owned Files Comparison (dev_sprint vs dev)

### 1. `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Comprehensive graph entity validation module (~250 lines). Defines canonical node types (`target`, `driver`, `indicator`, `information`), legacy aliases, valid endpoint pairs for CONDITIONAL and REFERENCE edges, and valid reference kinds. The main function `validate_graph_entities()` checks: persisted question nodes, qnode-as-driver scaffolding, unknown node types, duplicate target options, invalid edge endpoint pairs, shared indicators across multiple drivers, and incomplete driver-target fanout in "full" graph mode. Supports `graph_kind` parameter (`generic`, `driver_indicator`, `full`).

**What exists on dev:** Does not exist.

**Key gaps:** Entirely new file. Must be created on dev. Imports `EdgeRecord` and `NodeRecord` from `packages.storage`.

---

### 2. `packages/inference/llm/litellm_preflight.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** LiteLLM provider preflight check module (~220 lines). Validates proxy readiness, model alias registration, and performs tiny completion probes against configured models. Supports both tier-based and task-based probe modes. Detects provider auth failures (including OpenRouter-specific markers). Exports `run_litellm_preflight`, `LiteLLMPreflightError`, `ProviderAuthError`. Re-exported from `packages/inference/llm/__init__.py` on dev_sprint.

**What exists on dev:** Does not exist. Not exported from `packages/inference/llm/__init__.py` on dev.

**Key gaps:** Entirely new file. The `__init__.py` of the LLM package on dev_sprint adds the import/export of this module. Depends on `packages.inference.llm.tiers` (tier resolution, model set loading).

---

### 3. `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Driver indicator literature retrieval module (~490 lines). Runs per-driver evidence retrieval and produces per-driver result files.

**What exists on dev:** Same module exists (~360 lines), with less functionality.

**Key differences (dev_sprint adds):**
1. **Lazy imports:** Top-level imports of `run_agent_single_scope_retrieval`, `LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent`, config classes are replaced with lazy-loading via `_get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval()` and `_get_retrieval_components()` -- avoids circular import issues.
2. **`_selected_sources_from_driver_refs()` function (new):** Carries grounded driver evidence titles (from `pdf_refs` and `source_contexts`) into indicator extraction when per-driver retrieval is empty. Deduplicates by source key.
3. **Short-circuit with driver refs:** Before attempting live retrieval, checks if the driver already has grounded refs. If so, skips the retrieval call entirely and returns a result row from driver refs alone.
4. **Post-retrieval fallback to driver refs:** If retrieval returns empty `selected_sources`, falls back to `_selected_sources_from_driver_refs()`.
5. **`driver_id` in result rows:** The `_driver_indicator_result_row` function adds a `driver_id` field.
6. **Thread-safe concurrency:** Uses `submit_with_context` from `packages.inference.llm.concurrency` instead of raw `executor.submit` for thread pool work.

---

### 4. `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Indicator extraction from driver literature using Claude API (~480 lines). Extracts market-observable indicators via LLM.

**What exists on dev:** Same module exists (~470 lines), with structural differences.

**Key differences (dev_sprint changes):**
1. **System prompt:** Hardcoded `_SYSTEM_PROMPT` string instead of loading from prompt store via `_load_system_prompt()` / `default_prompt_store().load("FS3/indicator_extraction/system.md")`.
2. **Model resolution import:** Uses `from packages.inference.llm import resolve_task_model` instead of `from packages.inference.llm.tiers import resolve_task_model`.
3. **Exported constant:** Adds `INDICATOR_EXTRACTION_MODEL = resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")` at module level for use by tests.
4. **Proxy-aware cache control:** System message `cache_control` is conditionally omitted when `LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL` is set (proxy doesn't support ephemeral caching). Dev always includes it.
5. **`DriverIndicatorExtractionTool.run()` method:** Uses `self._runtime.complete_structured()` (structured output via LLM runtime) instead of calling `extract_causal_relationships()` then `validate_extraction_response()` sequentially. This routes through the shared proxy-safe LLM runtime.
6. **`system_prompt` as class attribute:** `_SYSTEM_PROMPT` assigned as class attribute rather than loaded in `__init__`.

---

### 5. `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Indicator-to-driver graph application (~810 lines). Creates indicator nodes and CONDITIONAL edges, linking indicators to parent drivers. Also creates REFERENCE edges from information nodes to indicators.

**What exists on dev:** Same module exists (~560 lines), simpler version.

**Key differences (dev_sprint adds):**
1. **`reference_edge_type` config field:** `IndicatorGraphApplicationConfig` adds `reference_edge_type: str = "REFERENCE"`.
2. **Driver-scoped indicator identity:** New `_indicator_identity_key(driver_id, indicator_label)` and `_legacy_indicator_identity_key()` functions. Indicators are now scoped per-driver rather than globally deduplicated by name. Same indicator name under different drivers creates separate nodes.
3. **`by_id` lookup in `_driver_nodes_index`:** Returns 3-tuple `(by_title, by_query, by_id)` instead of 2-tuple. Enables matching drivers by their graph node ID directly (`driver_id` / `graph_driver_id` from the literature row).
4. **`_existing_indicator_nodes` uses driver-scoped keys:** Looks up `parent_driver_id` in indicator detail to build scoped identity keys.
5. **Information-to-indicator REFERENCE edges (new):** New functions `_source_key()`, `_source_keys_from_payload()`, `_information_source_lookup()`, `_resolved_information_ids_for_sources()`, `_indicator_is_inferred()`, `_create_indicator_reference_edges()`. These create REFERENCE edges from existing information/evidence nodes to newly created indicator nodes, grounding indicators in their source documents.
6. **`_indicator_detail` gains `parent_driver_id` and `parent_driver_label`:** Indicator nodes carry their parent driver identity.
7. **Stats tracking:** `_IndicatorApplyStats` adds `indicator_reference_edges_added` and `indicator_reference_edges_skipped`.
8. **`_collect_eligible_drivers` gains `driver_id_lookup`:** Matches by `driver_id`/`graph_driver_id` before falling back to title/query matching.
9. **`_upsert_indicator_node` gains `driver_id` and `driver_title` params:** For driver-scoped indicator creation.
10. **Revised stage outcome logic:** `_compute_indicator_stage_outcome` reorders conditions -- `review_required_no_candidates` is checked before `drivers_without_indicators`; `missing_driver_rows` without `drivers_without_indicators` yields `partial` (not `failed`).
11. **`_build_driver_summary` adds `reference_edges_added`.**
12. **Import of `document_review` module:** For `canonical_document_key_from_source` and `document_key_from_node` used in source key resolution.

---

### 6. `tests/test_live_graph_entity_process.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** End-to-end live graph entity validation test (~281 lines). Contains a gated live test (`test_live_fs1_4_question_process_produces_valid_graph_entities`) that runs a full research driver pipeline on a seeded FS1-FS4 question and validates the output graph against the entity validation rules. Also has a unit test (`test_graph_gate_validation_uses_run_scoped_graph_rows`) for run-scoped graph row validation. Uses `validate_graph_entities`, `run_litellm_preflight`, seed tooling, and the full research driver pipeline.

**What exists on dev:** Does not exist.

**Key gaps:** Entirely new file. Heavy integration test with many dependencies: `packages.domain.graph_entity_validation`, `packages.inference.llm.run_litellm_preflight`, `packages.storage`, `tools.cli.commands`, `tools.cli.fs1_4_seed`, `services.worker.research_driver_question_pipeline`.

---

### 7. `tests/test_graph_entity_validation.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Unit tests for the graph entity validation module (~155 lines). Tests canonical endpoint acceptance, information-target reference rejection, qnode-as-driver + driver-target reference edge rejection, question nodes + duplicate targets, shared indicator across multiple drivers, full graph complete/incomplete driver-target fanout, and driver-indicator graph slice acceptance.

**What exists on dev:** Does not exist.

**Key gaps:** Entirely new file. Depends on `packages.domain.graph_entity_validation` (also new).

---

### 8. `tests/test_graph_entity_conformance.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Cross-layer conformance tests (~135 lines). Verifies that database enums (`NodeType`, `EdgeType`, `ReferenceKind`) match the canonical graph entity definitions. Tests that the API golden graph output (`tests/golden/graph_bundle.json`) drift is recorded in a conformance report. Validates that the conformance report (`docs/plans/definitions/graph-entity-conformance-report.md`) tracks cross-layer blockers in schema files, repository code, and frontend theme/cytoscape styles.

**What exists on dev:** Does not exist.

**Key gaps:** Entirely new file. Depends on `packages.domain.graph_entity_validation`, `packages.storage.database` enums, golden fixture file `tests/golden/graph_bundle.json`, conformance report doc, and several other repo artifacts. This test will need careful evaluation of which dependencies exist on dev.

---

### 9. `tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Extensive test suite for indicator graph application (~1548 lines).

**What exists on dev:** Same file exists (~1165 lines), with fewer tests.

**Key differences (dev_sprint adds ~383 net lines):**
1. **`INDICATOR_EXTRACTION_MODEL` import** instead of `resolve_task_model` from `packages.inference.llm.tiers`.
2. **`test_apply_indicators_links_source_backed_information_to_indicator`:** New test verifying information-to-indicator REFERENCE edge creation.
3. **`test_apply_indicators_marks_all_review_required_no_candidate_rows_as_partial`:** New test for the revised stage outcome logic where all review_required rows with no candidates yield `partial` status.
4. **`test_apply_indicators_marks_missing_driver_nodes_as_partial_when_matched_rows_succeed`:** New test for the case where some driver rows can't be matched but matched ones succeed -- overall status is `partial` with reason `indicator_missing_driver_nodes`.
5. **`test_apply_indicators_matches_graph_admitted_driver_id_before_title`:** New test verifying that `driver_id` matching takes precedence over title matching.
6. **`test_claude_api_mock_end_to_end` revised:** Mocks `LLMRuntime.complete_structured` instead of the raw `_get_litellm_completion_fn` / anthropic SDK boundary. Validates the `INDICATOR_EXTRACTION_MODEL` instead of `resolve_task_model`.
7. **`test_shared_indicator_across_drivers_creates_one_node_two_edges` renamed** to `test_same_indicator_name_across_drivers_creates_driver_scoped_nodes`: Updated expectations -- same indicator name now creates 2 nodes (one per driver) instead of 1, with `parent_driver_id` and `indicator_scope` in detail.
8. **Assertion additions:** `indicator_reference_edges_added` count checked in existing tests.

---

### 10. `tests/test_driver_indicator_literature.py`

**What it does on dev_sprint:** Tests for driver indicator literature retrieval (~315 lines).

**What exists on dev:** Same file exists (~236 lines), with fewer tests.

**Key differences (dev_sprint adds ~79 net lines):**
1. **`test_driver_indicator_literature_uses_driver_refs_without_indicator_retrieval`:** New test verifying that when a driver has `pdf_refs`, the module short-circuits retrieval and uses those refs as selected sources directly. Confirms no retrieval calls are made, deduplication works, and `source_mode` is `driver_extraction_pdf_ref`.

```
**1. `packages/inference/llm/concurrency.py`** -- YES, exists on dev. Exports both `bind_current_context` and `submit_with_context` (listed in `__all__`). It preserves `ContextVar` state across thread-pool boundaries.

**2. `core/research_driver/document_review.py`** -- YES, exists on dev. Both `canonical_document_key_from_source` and `document_key_from_node` are defined in it.

**3. `packages/inference/llm/__init__.py`** -- YES, `resolve_task_model` is exported. It is re-exported from `.tiers`. The full `__all__` list includes: `BaseAgent`, `BaseLLMTool`, `DEFAULT_MODEL_SET_NAME`, `DEFAULT_PROFILE_NAME`, `LLMConfig`, `LLMPolicy`, `LLMModelSetCapabilities`, `LLMTier`, `LLMRuntime`, `TIER_LARGE`, `TIER_MEDIUM`, `TIER_SMALL`, `ZERO_USAGE`, `build_llm_config_snapshot`, `llm_profile_context`, `llm_trace_context`, `load_llm_task_definitions`, `load_llm_profile_capabilities`, `Message`, `model_set_context`, `ModelProfiles`, `resolve_llm_profile_capabilities`, `resolve_llm_profile_name`, `resolve_model_set_name`, `resolve_role_model`, `resolve_task_model`, `resolve_task_tier`, `StepCostAccumulator`, `UsageTotals`, `run_tool_batch`, `step_cost_context`, `sum_usage`.

**4. `packages/storage/database.py`** -- YES, all three enums exist on dev:
- `NodeType`: `TARGET`, `DRIVER`, `INDICATOR`, `INFORMATION`
- `EdgeType`: `CONDITIONAL`, `REFERENCE`
- `ReferenceKind`: `SOURCE`, `EVIDENCE`, `HISTORIC_DATA`

**5. `tests/golden/graph_bundle.json`** -- YES, exists on dev. Contains edges and nodes with types like `"conditional"`, `"reference"`, and node data.

**6. `packages/inference/llm/runtime.py`** -- YES, exists on dev. `LLMRuntime` class is defined and has a `complete_structured` method.

**7. `tools/cli/fs1_4_seed.py`** -- NO, does not exist on dev. No seed files exist anywhere under `tools/`. Seed-related files are in `ops/seed/` (SQL seed scripts and `seed_live.py`) and `fs10_validation/seed/`.

**8. `packages/inference/llm/tiers.py`** -- YES, `resolve_task_model` is defined here (it's the canonical source; `__init__.py` re-exports it). Other model-set/profile functions on dev:
- `model_set_context`, `llm_profile_context` (context managers)
- `resolve_model_set_name`, `resolve_model_set_selection`
- `resolve_llm_profile_name`, `resolve_llm_profile_selection`, `resolve_llm_profile_capabilities`
- `load_llm_tier_profiles`, `load_llm_profile_capabilities`, `load_llm_task_definitions`
- `resolve_tier_model`, `resolve_role_model`, `resolve_task_tier`, `resolve_task_model`
- `build_llm_config_snapshot`, `resolved_expected_litellm_models`
- Helper classes: `LLMTier` (StrEnum), `LLMModelSetSelection`, `LLMModelSetCapabilities`, `LLMTaskDefinition`

**9. `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py`** -- There is no function named `_node_type_for_record`, but the equivalent logic exists. Here is how types are handled:

- **Nodes**: A `_NODE_TYPE_MAP` dict maps string labels to `NodeType` enum values. The function `_node_record_to_row` uses `_NODE_TYPE_MAP.get(raw_type, NodeType.DRIVER)` -- unknown node types silently default to `NodeType.DRIVER`. The map includes aliases: `"option"` -> `TARGET`, `"evidence"` / `"evidence_price_data"` / `"historic_event_set"` -> `INFORMATION`, `"question"` -> `DRIVER`.

- **Edges**: `_edge_record_to_row` exists. It handles unknown edge types by defaulting anything that is not `"CONDITIONAL"` (case-insensitive upper) to `EdgeType.REFERENCE`. The logic is: `EdgeType.CONDITIONAL if raw_type == "CONDITIONAL" else EdgeType.REFERENCE`.
```

## Phase 2: Harmonization — SPI-228 (P-E2E-GRAPH-01)

### dev_sprint vs design docs vs current dev

**Design docs define P-E2E-GRAPH-01 as:**
- Live complete DB-backed run returning and persisting a compliant graph
- Canonical node types: `target`, `driver`, `indicator`, `information`
- Canonical edge types: `CONDITIONAL`, `REFERENCE`
- REFERENCE edges: only `source` or `evidence` relationship kinds
- `validate_graph_entities()` must report issue_count=0 on both returned and persisted graphs
- Deterministic fixtures do NOT earn the port tick

**dev_sprint implementation aligns with design docs** on all owned files. No contradictions found between the validator logic and the graph-entity-definitions doc.

### Harmonization Findings

**Finding 1: Massive cross-cutting scope in shared files**

The pipeline file (`research_driver_question_pipeline.py`) has ~855 lines of diff on dev_sprint, but only ~30 lines are the actual graph gate repair:
- `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()` — filters reduced drivers through graph delta's admitted list
- `_has_indicator_literature_sources()` — checks driver has PDF refs or source contexts
- Step 4 change: raw `reduced_drivers` → filtered list
- `"graph_delta"` key in extraction debug output

The rest is FS2 evidence chains, Brier scores, run-config threading, QC DB reads, per-step cost breakdowns — all out of SPI-228 scope.

Similarly, `commands.py` and `parser.py` have huge diffs (run-config system, FS10/FS12 subcommands, storage-init bootstrap) but only the `model_overrides` → `model_set` transition is graph-adjacent.

**Finding 2: `tools/cli/fs1_4_seed.py` doesn't exist on dev**

The live integration test (`test_live_graph_entity_process.py`) imports `tools.cli.fs1_4_seed`. This file doesn't exist on dev. Seed files live in `ops/seed/` on dev. We'd need to either port this file or adapt the test.

**Finding 3: Repository coercion — flagged but out of scope**

The conformance report flags `sql_repositories.py`: unknown node types silently default to `DRIVER`, unknown edge types default to `REFERENCE`. Design says reject, not coerce. But this file is NOT owned by SPI-228, and changing it could break other flows. The validator catches these issues at the boundary instead.

**Finding 4: `HISTORIC_DATA` reference kind in DB enum**

`packages/storage/database.py` has `ReferenceKind.HISTORIC_DATA` which isn't in the validator's canonical set (`source`, `evidence`). The validator doesn't currently check reference kinds — it focuses on node types and edge endpoint pairs. This is a pre-existing gap, not introduced by this port.

**Finding 5: Cross-cutting dependencies all exist on dev**

Good news — all needed imports are already available on dev:
- `submit_with_context` from `packages.inference.llm.concurrency` ✓
- `canonical_document_key_from_source`, `document_key_from_node` from `core.research_driver.document_review` ✓
- `LLMRuntime.complete_structured()` ✓
- `resolve_task_model` ✓
- `model_set_context` ✓
- DB enums (`NodeType`, `EdgeType`, `ReferenceKind`) ✓

---

## Phase 3: Proposed Approach

**Port all 5 owned source files** from dev_sprint (4 new, 1 modified substantially):
1. `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py` — NEW, the validator
2. `packages/inference/llm/litellm_preflight.py` — NEW, provider preflight
3. `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py` — lazy imports, driver-ref short-circuit, submit_with_context
4. `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py` — hardcoded system prompt, proxy cache control, complete_structured routing
5. `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py` — driver-scoped indicators, REFERENCE edges, driver-id matching, revised stage outcomes

**Port all 5 test files** (3 new, 2 modified):
6. `tests/test_graph_entity_validation.py` — NEW
7. `tests/test_graph_entity_conformance.py` — NEW
8. `tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py` — new tests + revised expectations
9. `tests/test_driver_indicator_literature.py` — new driver-ref test
10. `tests/test_live_graph_entity_process.py` — NEW (will need seed path adaptation)

**Minimal shared-file changes:**
11. `config/llm_tasks.toml` — `FS3.indicator_extraction` tier `large` → `medium`, add 2 new entries
12. `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py` — ONLY the graph gate repair functions + Step 4 change + `graph_delta` key (not FS2/Brier/run-config/cost)

**Scope decisions I need your call on:**

1. **CLI files** — The `model_overrides` → `model_set` transition in `commands.py`/`parser.py` is needed for the indicator extraction tier to resolve correctly via `model_set_context`. Port this minimal subset, or defer to a cross-cutting ticket?

2. **`tools/cli/fs1_4_seed.py`** — Doesn't exist on dev. Port it from dev_sprint, or adapt the live test to use existing `ops/seed/` infrastructure?

3. **Repository coercion** (`sql_repositories.py` defaulting unknown types) — Leave as-is for SPI-228 and note as out-of-scope follow-up?

4. **Pipeline scope boundary** — The pipeline has ~30 lines of core graph repair vs ~825 lines of other changes. Confirm: only port the graph-admitted-drivers filter + `graph_delta` key, leave everything else (FS2 evidence, Brier, run-config, QC DB reads, cost breakdowns) for their respective tickets?

| Out-of-scope change in pipeline/CLI | Covered by | Ticket |
|---|---|---|
| FS2 evidence chains (`_sync_fs2_document_blob_authority`, `_sync_fs2_runtime_authority`) | **SPI-231** — Port FS2 runtime, current-resolution, and Research Defensibility bridge | Lane 3 |
| Brier score computation (`_compute_graph_brier_metrics`, `_resolved_question_truth`) | **SPI-235** — Port FS6 inference and FS7 output validation surfaces | Lane 3 (FS6 owns posterior/calibration) |
| Run-config propagation (`run_config_id`, `config_snapshot`, run-config CLI) | **SPI-236** — Port API auth, job/run-config propagation, and worker context | Lane 4 |
| QC DB reads (`_load_qc_steps_payload`) | **SPI-229** — Port FS11 QC infrastructure | Lane 3 |
| Per-step LLM cost breakdowns in `save_run_steps` | **SPI-226** — Port prompt/LLM infrastructure (already landed) or **SPI-236** (worker context) | Landed / Lane 4 |
| CLI: FS10/FS12 subcommands (validation, tuning, polymarket, pricing) | **SPI-230** — Port FS10 validation lifecycle | Lane 3 |
| CLI: `model_overrides` → `model_set` transition | **SPI-236** — run-config propagation | Lane 4 |
| `tools/cli/fs1_4_seed.py` | **SPI-237** — Port ops, LiteLLM, Docker, and seed changes | Lane 5 |
| Repository coercion fix (`sql_repositories.py` defaulting unknown types) | **SPI-232** — Port FS3 extraction, graph admission (owns graph admission layer) | Lane 1 |

**Everything has a ticket.** No new tickets needed.

The shared-file coordination is already called out in the tickets too — e.g., SPI-231 explicitly lists `research_driver_question_pipeline.py` as shared with SPI-228, and SPI-230 lists `commands.py`/`parser.py` as shared with SPI-228.

---

So back to the scope decisions with this confirmed:

1. **CLI `model_overrides` → `model_set`** — this belongs to SPI-236. For SPI-228 I'll keep the existing `model_overrides` interface in the CLI and only change the owned files. The indicator extraction module can resolve its own task model internally without needing the CLI to pass `model_set`.

2. **`tools/cli/fs1_4_seed.py`** — belongs to SPI-237 (Lane 5). For the live integration test, I'll either skip the seed import gracefully or mark the live test as gated on SPI-237 landing.

3. **Pipeline file** — only the ~30 lines of graph gate repair (`_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers`, `_has_indicator_literature_sources`, Step 4 filter, `graph_delta` key). All other pipeline changes belong to their respective tickets.

4. **Repository coercion** — belongs to SPI-232, leave as-is.

Want me to proceed with implementation on this scoping, or do you want to adjust anything?

```
Now I have a complete picture. Let me summarize my findings.

---

Here is the file-by-file analysis of what minimal shared-file changes are needed for SPI-228's owned files to work.

## 1. `packages/inference/llm/__init__.py` -- Re-export of `litellm_preflight`

**Not needed for SPI-228.** None of the 10 owned files import `litellm_preflight`, `LiteLLMPreflightError`, `ProviderAuthError`, or `run_litellm_preflight`. The file `litellm_preflight.py` already exists on dev but is not re-exported from `__init__.py`. Only `tests/test_live_graph_entity_process.py` (not an owned file) imports it.

The owned `driver_indicator_literature.py` imports `submit_with_context` from `packages.inference.llm.concurrency`, and that module already exists on dev. The owned `indicator_extraction.py` imports `resolve_task_model` from `packages.inference.llm`, and that's already exported on dev.

**Verdict: No changes needed to `packages/inference/llm/__init__.py` for SPI-228.**

## 2. `config/llm_tasks.toml` -- Tier change for `FS3.indicator_extraction`

On dev: `FS3.indicator_extraction` tier = `"large"`
On dev_sprint: `FS3.indicator_extraction` tier = `"medium"`

The owned `indicator_extraction.py` calls `resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")` twice (once at module level for the compatibility alias `INDICATOR_EXTRACTION_MODEL`, once in `extract_causal_relationships`, and once in `DriverIndicatorExtractionTool.__init__`). This resolves at runtime from the TOML file. The code works with either tier -- it just picks a different model.

No other new task IDs from dev_sprint (`FS3.indicator_deduplication_prompt`, `FS3.driver_indicator_network_tuning`, `FS4.fermi_*`) are referenced by any owned files.

**Verdict: Change tier from `"large"` to `"medium"` for `FS3.indicator_extraction` in `config/llm_tasks.toml`. This is a one-line change. The code is functionally correct either way, but the tier change is the intended behavior from dev_sprint.**

## 3. `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py` -- Pipeline changes

This is the most significant shared-file change area. On dev_sprint, three key changes were made:

### 3a. `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()` function (new, ~40 lines)
- Added at line 615 on dev_sprint
- Filters `reduced_drivers` to only those admitted to the graph via `graph_delta`, enriches each with `driver_id` and `graph_driver_title`, and filters out drivers without indicator literature sources
- Called at the call site (line 2770 on dev_sprint) replacing the old inline list comprehension:
  - **Dev:** `reduced_list = [x for x in (drivers_payload.get("reduced_drivers") or []) if isinstance(x, dict)]`
  - **Dev_sprint:** `reduced_list = _graph_admitted_reduced_drivers(drivers_payload)`

### 3b. `_has_indicator_literature_sources()` function (new, ~15 lines)
- Helper for `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers` that checks if a driver has pdf_refs or source_contexts

### 3c. `"graph_delta"` key in `drivers_payload` (1-line addition)
- On dev_sprint, `drivers_payload` now includes `"graph_delta": dict(extraction_debug.get("graph_delta") or {})` (at line 1744 in the `_run_driver_extraction_stage` function)
- On dev, this key is absent from `drivers_payload`
- `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers` reads `drivers_payload.get("graph_delta")` and gracefully falls back to returning all reduced_drivers if it's missing

**However**, the owned `driver_indicator_literature.py` already has its own driver-ref short-circuit (line 388-405: if `_selected_sources_from_driver_refs(driver)` returns results, it skips retrieval entirely). And the owned `indicator_graph_apply.py` already handles `driver_id` matching via `_driver_nodes_index` (by_id lookup). So the pipeline changes make the pipeline *smarter* about which drivers to send to indicator literature, but the owned files are defensive enough to work without these pipeline changes.

**What's strictly required for the owned files to be callable:**
- The owned `driver_indicator_literature.py`'s `run_driver_indicator_literature_for_question()` accepts `storage_adapters` and `graph_store_paths` parameters. These are already passed by the pipeline on dev via `_run_indicator_literature_stage` -> `_DriverIndicatorsRequest` -> `ResearchTool._run_driver_indicators()`.
- The owned `indicator_graph_apply.py`'s `apply_indicators_from_driver_literature()` accepts the same parameters as on dev. No new parameters needed.

**What's needed for full dev_sprint behavior:**
1. Add `"graph_delta"` to `drivers_payload` in `_run_driver_extraction_stage` (1 line)
2. Add `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()` function (~40 lines)
3. Add `_has_indicator_literature_sources()` function (~15 lines)
4. Change the `reduced_list` assignment at the call site (1 line)

**Verdict: The owned files are importable and testable without any pipeline changes. For full behavioral parity with dev_sprint, the pipeline needs `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers`, `_has_indicator_literature_sources`, the `graph_delta` key in `drivers_payload`, and the call site change. But these are not required for the owned files to compile, import, or pass their unit tests.**

## 4. `tools/cli/commands.py` and `tools/cli/parser.py`

**No changes needed.** None of the 10 owned files or their 3 tests import from `tools.cli.commands` or `tools.cli.parser`. Confirmed via grep.

## Summary of minimal shared-file changes

| Shared file | Change needed | Priority | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| `config/llm_tasks.toml` | Change `FS3.indicator_extraction` tier from `"large"` to `"medium"` | Required for behavioral parity | Owned `indicator_extraction.py` uses this task ID; wrong tier means wrong model |
| `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py` | Add `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()`, `_has_indicator_literature_sources()`, `graph_delta` to `drivers_payload`, change call site | Required for full feature | Pipeline is the caller; without these, the graph-admitted driver filtering (the "graph gate repair" from SPI-228's name) doesn't actually happen at the pipeline level |
| `packages/inference/llm/__init__.py` | None | N/A | No owned files need new re-exports |
| `tools/cli/commands.py` | None | N/A | Not imported by any owned files |
| `tools/cli/parser.py` | None | N/A | Not imported by any owned files |
```

```
31	def _node_type(node: NodeRecord) -> str:
32	    raw_type = str(node.type or "").strip().lower()
33	    return {"option": "target", "evidence": "information"}.get(raw_type, raw_type)

36	def _edge_type(edge: EdgeRecord) -> str:

40	def _node_question_id(node: NodeRecord) -> str:
41	    return str((node.data or {}).get("question_id") or "").strip()

44	def _node_detail(node: NodeRecord) -> dict[str, Any]:
45	    data = dict(node.data or {})
46	    detail = data.get("detail")
47	    return dict(detail) if isinstance(detail, dict) else data
48

50	def _maybe_float(value: Any) -> float | None:
51	    if value in (None, ""):
52	        return None
53	    try:
54	        return float(value)
55	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
56	        return None

59	def _records_from_graph_objects(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[list[NodeRecord], list[EdgeRecord]]:
60	    nodes = [
61	        NodeRecord(
62	            node_id=str(row.get("id") or "").strip(),
63	            type=str(row.get("type") or "").strip(),
64	            label=str(row.get("label") or "").strip(),
65	            toi=None,
66	            baseline_p=_maybe_float(row.get("baseline_p")),
67	            posterior_p=_maybe_float(row.get("posterior_p")),
68	            data=dict(row.get("data") or {}),
69	        )
70	        for row in payload.get("nodes") or []
71	        if isinstance(row, dict) and str(row.get("id") or "").strip()
72	    ]
73	    edges = [
74	        EdgeRecord(
75	            edge_id=str(row.get("id") or "").strip(),
76	            from_node=str(row.get("from_id") or "").strip(),
77	            to_node=str(row.get("to_id") or "").strip(),
78	            toi=None,
79	            weight=_maybe_float(row.get("weight")),
80	            type=str(row.get("type") or "").strip(),
81	            data=dict(row.get("data") or {}),
82	        )
83	        for row in payload.get("edges") or []
84	        if isinstance(row, dict) and str(row.get("id") or "").strip()
85	    ]
86	    return nodes, edges
87

89	def _driver_indicator_subgraph(nodes: list[NodeRecord], edges: list[EdgeRecord]) -> tuple[list[NodeRecord], list[EdgeRecord]]:
90	    node_by_id = {str(node.node_id): node for node in nodes}
91	    selected_node_ids: set[str] = set()
92	    for edge in edges:
93	        from_node = node_by_id.get(str(edge.from_node))
94	        to_node = node_by_id.get(str(edge.to_node))
95	        if from_node is None or to_node is None:
96	            continue
97	        pair = (_node_type(from_node), _node_type(to_node))
98	        if _edge_type(edge) == "CONDITIONAL" and pair == ("indicator", "driver"):
99	            selected_node_ids.update({str(edge.from_node), str(edge.to_node)})
100	        elif _edge_type(edge) == "REFERENCE" and pair in {("information", "indicator"), ("information", "driver")}:
101	            selected_node_ids.update({str(edge.from_node), str(edge.to_node)})
102	
103	    selected_nodes = [node for node in nodes if str(node.node_id) in selected_node_ids and _node_type(node) != "target"]
104	    selected_edges = [edge for edge in edges if str(edge.from_node) in selected_node_ids and str(edge.to_node) in selected_node_ids]
105	    return selected_nodes, selected_edges

108	def _assert_required_live_graph_shape(*, nodes: list[NodeRecord], edges: list[EdgeRecord], question: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
109	    question_id = str(question.get("id") or "").strip()
110	    options = [str(option).strip() for option in question.get("options") or [] if str(option).strip()]
111	    node_by_id = {str(node.node_id): node for node in nodes}
112	
113	    target_nodes = [node for node in nodes if _node_type(node) == "target" and (not question_id or _node_question_id(node) == question_id)]
114	    target_indices = {_node_detail(node).get("option_index") for node in target_nodes}
115	    assert len(target_nodes) == len(options), f"expected {len(options)} target nodes, found {len(target_nodes)}"
116	    assert target_indices == set(range(len(options))), f"target option indices did not match question options: {target_indices}"
117	
118	    driver_nodes = [node for node in nodes if _node_type(node) == "driver" and (not question_id or _node_question_id(node) == question_id)]
119	    indicator_nodes = [
120	        node for node in nodes if _node_type(node) == "indicator" and (not question_id or _node_question_id(node) == question_id)
121	    ]
122	    assert driver_nodes, "live graph produced no driver nodes"
123	    assert indicator_nodes, "live graph produced no indicator nodes"
124	
125	    indicator_driver_edges = []
126	    driver_target_edges_by_driver: dict[str, set[str]] = defaultdict(set)
127	    target_ids = {str(node.node_id) for node in target_nodes}
128	    for edge in edges:
129	        from_node = node_by_id.get(str(edge.from_node))
130	        to_node = node_by_id.get(str(edge.to_node))
131	        if from_node is None or to_node is None or _edge_type(edge) != "CONDITIONAL":
132	            continue
133	        pair = (_node_type(from_node), _node_type(to_node))
134	        if pair == ("indicator", "driver"):
135	            indicator_driver_edges.append(edge)
136	        elif pair == ("driver", "target"):
137	            driver_target_edges_by_driver[str(edge.from_node)].add(str(edge.to_node))
138	
139	    assert indicator_driver_edges, "live graph produced no indicator -> driver CONDITIONAL edges"
140	    assert any(target_ids and target_ids <= covered_target_ids for covered_target_ids in driver_target_edges_by_driver.values()), (
141	        "live graph produced no complete driver -> target target-space fanout"
142	    )
```

```
def _run_driver_extraction_stage(
    *,
    components: ResearchDriverQuestionPipelineComponents,
    graph: Any,
    question_id: str,
    question_dir: Path,
    drivers_path: Path,
    budgets_payload: dict[str, Any],
    resolved_budgets: Any,
    routing_payload: dict[str, Any],
    include_debug_artifacts: bool,
    min_factors: int,
    max_factors: int,
    target_toi: datetime,
    model_overrides: dict[str, str] | None,
    artifact_repository: ArtifactRepository = json_artifact_repository,
) -> _ExtractionStageResult:
    """Run Agent02 extraction/reduction and persist driver/debug payloads."""
    t_extract = time.perf_counter()
    chunk_debug_dir = question_dir / "debug" / "chunks"
    insight = components.driver_extractor_agent_factory.create(
        graph=graph,
        question_id=question_id,
        resolved_budgets=resolved_budgets,
        fallback_pack_name=str(routing_payload.get("pack_name") or "generic_arxiv"),
        include_debug_artifacts=bool(include_debug_artifacts),
        chunk_debug_dir=chunk_debug_dir,
        run_id=f"{question_id}-{int(time.time())}",
        chunk_map_model=_model_override(model_overrides, "chunk_map"),
        reduce_model=_model_override(model_overrides, "reduce"),
        native_model=_model_override(model_overrides, "insight_native"),
        artifact_repository=artifact_repository,
    )
    graph = insight.run(
        min_factors=int(min_factors),
        max_factors=int(max_factors),
        toi=target_toi,
    )
```

```
def run_research_driver_for_question(
    *,
    question: dict[str, Any],
    graphs_dir: Path,
    questions_path: Path | None = None,
    run_dir: Path,
    run_id: str | None = None,
    graph_id: str,
    pdfs_per_scope: int | None,
    feedthrough: int | None,
    chunk_size: int | None,
    max_online_pages: int | None,
    native_max_chunk_contexts: int | None,
    native_max_grounded_factors: int | None,
    native_max_free_factors: int | None,
    native_max_existing_candidates: int | None,
    native_max_output_tokens: int | None,
    min_factors: int,
    max_factors: int,
    clear_existing_pdfs: bool,
    use_llm_query_boost: bool,
    include_debug_artifacts: bool,
    local_repo_path: Path | None = None,
    prefer_local_repo: bool = False,
    driver_indicator_enabled: bool,
    driver_indicator_pdfs_per_driver: int | None,
    driver_indicator_feedthrough: int | None,
    driver_indicator_max_drivers: int | None,
    indicator_max_per_driver: int | None,
    qc_enabled: bool = True,
    qc_capture_prompts: bool = True,
    qc_include_chunk_text: bool = False,
    validate_store_on_persist: bool = True,
    question_summary_enabled: bool = True,
    question_summary_llm_enabled: bool = True,
    question_summary_max_output_tokens: int = 4000,
    model_overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    model_set: str | None = None,
    llm_profile: str | None = None,
    run_config_id: str | None = None,
    run_config_snapshot: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    filter_mode: str | None = None,
    components: ResearchDriverQuestionPipelineComponents | None = None,
    storage_adapters: WorkerStorageAdapters | None = None,
    graph_store_paths: GraphStorePaths | None = None,
    progress_callback: Callable[..., None] | None = None,
    workflow_mode: str = WORKFLOW_MODE_FULL_RUN,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Run the per-question research-driver pipeline and write all artifacts.

    The function is intentionally split into the same `step_N_*` phases listed
    in `RESEARCH_DRIVER_QUESTION_PIPELINE_STEPS` for readability and review.
    """
    from core.research_driver.data_model import BayesianGraph

    components = components or build_default_question_pipeline_components()
    model_set = str(model_set or llm_profile or "").strip() or None
    resolved_storage = resolve_worker_storage_adapters(storage_adapters)
    artifact_repository = resolved_storage.artifact_repository
    resolved_graph_store_paths = graph_store_paths or resolve_graph_store_paths(
        graphs_dir=graphs_dir,
        questions_path=questions_path,
    )

    question_id = str(question.get("id") or "").strip()
    if not question_id:
        raise ValueError("Question missing id")
```

## 2. Add `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()` function

**Insert after** `_hydrate_driver_nodes_for_step` which ends at **line 546** (the closing of the function body).

**Insert location: after line 546, before line 548 (the blank line before `_resolved_budgets_namespace`)**

**New code (from dev_sprint lines 615-649):**
```python
def _graph_admitted_reduced_drivers(drivers_payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    reduced_drivers = [dict(row) for row in (drivers_payload.get("reduced_drivers") or []) if isinstance(row, dict)]
    graph_delta = drivers_payload.get("graph_delta")
    if not isinstance(graph_delta, Mapping):
        return reduced_drivers
    admitted_rows = [row for row in (graph_delta.get("drivers") or []) if isinstance(row, Mapping)]
    if not admitted_rows:
        return reduced_drivers
    admitted_by_source_index: dict[int, Mapping[str, Any]] = {}
    for row in admitted_rows:
        try:
            source_index = int(row.get("source_index") or 0)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            continue
        if source_index > 0:
            admitted_by_source_index[source_index] = row
    if not admitted_by_source_index:
        return reduced_drivers

    out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    for source_index, driver in enumerate(reduced_drivers, start=1):
        admitted = admitted_by_source_index.get(source_index)
        if not admitted:
            continue
        driver_id = str(admitted.get("driver_id") or "").strip()
        if not driver_id:
            continue
        enriched = dict(driver)
        enriched["driver_id"] = driver_id
        enriched["graph_driver_title"] = str(admitted.get("title") or "").strip()
        if not _has_indicator_literature_sources(enriched):
            continue
        out.append(enriched)
    return out
```

## 3. Add `_has_indicator_literature_sources()` function

**Insert immediately after `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers`** (which references it, so it must be defined before or after -- in dev_sprint it's defined right after).

**New code (from dev_sprint lines 651-664):**
```python
def _has_indicator_literature_sources(driver: Mapping[str, Any]) -> bool:
    for raw_ref in driver.get("pdf_refs") or []:
        if str(raw_ref or "").strip():
            return True
    source_contexts = driver.get("source_contexts") if isinstance(driver.get("source_contexts"), list) else []
    for context in source_contexts:
        if not isinstance(context, Mapping):
            continue
        if any(
            str(context.get(key) or "").strip() for key in ("document_title", "pdf_ref", "pdf_file", "document_key", "evidence_node_id")
        ):
            return True
    return False
```

**Note:** `_has_indicator_literature_sources` must be defined BEFORE `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers` since the latter calls it, OR Python will resolve it at call time (not definition time) since both are module-level functions. In dev_sprint, `_has_indicator_literature_sources` is defined AFTER `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers` (line 651 vs 615), which works fine because both are module-level functions and the call happens at runtime, not import time.

2. The wrapping location would be somewhere after the `model_set` variable is resolved and before the pipeline body executes. Looking at the function body starting at line 1909, the user would likely want something like adding `model_set = str(model_set or "").strip() or None` after `components = ...` (line 1911), then wrapping the remainder in a context manager.

However, since `model_set_context` is a context manager (from `packages/inference/llm/tiers.py` line 87), wrapping the entire pipeline body would require significant indentation changes to the ~1200-line function body. A more practical approach would be to enter/exit the context at the top of the function using `contextlib.ExitStack` or an early `__enter__`/`__exit__` pattern.

**Summary for change 6:** This change is NOT present in dev_sprint and would be a new addition. If you want to proceed, you would need to decide on the wrapping strategy given the massive function body. The `model_set_context` function is available at `packages.inference.llm.model_set_context`.

False\n\n\ndef _graph_admitted_reduced_drivers(drivers_payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Filter reduced drivers to only those admitted to the graph with literature sources.\"\"\"\n    reduced_drivers = [dict(row) for row in (drivers_payload.get(\"reduced_drivers\") or []) if isinstance(row, dict)]\n    graph_delta = drivers_payload.get(\"graph_delta\")\n    if int(row.get(\"source_index\") or 0)\n        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n            continue\n        if source_index > 0:\n            admitted_by_source_index[source_index] = row\n    if not admitted_by_source_index:\n        return reduced_drivers\n\n    out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for source_index, driver in enumerate(reduced_drivers, start=1):\n        admitted = admitted_by_source_index.get(source_index)\n        if not admitted:\n            continue\n        driver_id = str(admitted.get(\"driver_id\") or \"\").strip()\n        if not driver_id:\n            continue\n        enriched = dict(driver)\n        enriched[\"driver_id\"] = driver_id\n        enriched[\"graph_driver_title\"] = str(admitted.get(\"title\") or \"\").strip()\n        if not from __future__ import annotations

```
from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Literal

import httpx
import pytest

from packages.inference.llm import litellm_preflight
from packages.inference.llm.litellm_preflight import LiteLLMPreflightError, ProviderAuthError, run_litellm_preflight
from packages.inference.llm.tiers import LLMTaskDefinition, LLMTier


def _response(method: str, url: str, status_code: int = 200, *, json_payload: Mapping | None = None, text: str = "") -> httpx.Response:
    request = httpx.Request(method, url)
    if json_payload is not None:
        return httpx.Response(status_code, json=dict(json_payload), request=request)
    return httpx.Response(status_code, text=text, request=request)
```

> AGENT

## What SPI-228 does

The pipeline runs a research process per question: it extracts drivers (causal factors), then finds indicators (observable data points) for each driver, and builds a graph connecting them all. The graph has nodes (targets, drivers, indicators, information sources) and edges (CONDITIONAL for causal links, REFERENCE for provenance).

**The problem on dev:** There's no validation that the graph coming out is actually well-formed. The pipeline sends *all* reduced drivers to indicator literature search, even ones that weren't admitted to the graph or have no literature backing. And there's no way to check that the final graph only contains canonical entity types.

**What this port fixes:**
1. Adds a graph entity validator that enforces the canonical rules
2. Adds provider preflight checks (is LiteLLM actually up before we run?)
3. Filters drivers so only graph-admitted ones with real literature sources go through indicator extraction
4. Makes indicator identity driver-scoped (same indicator name under two different drivers = two separate nodes)
5. Creates REFERENCE edges from information nodes to indicators (provenance trail)

---

## File-by-file walkthrough

### New modules

**`packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py`** — The validator. Defines the canonical rules:
- Node types: `target`, `driver`, `indicator`, `information` (aliases `option`→target, `evidence`→information)
- Edge endpoint pairs: which node types can connect via CONDITIONAL vs REFERENCE
- Checks for: question nodes masquerading as drivers, duplicate target options, invalid edge pairs, shared indicators across drivers, incomplete driver→target fanout
- Returns a list of typed issues with codes like `"invalid_reference_kind"`, `"duplicate_target_option"`

**`packages/inference/llm/litellm_preflight.py`** — Pre-run sanity check. Pings the LiteLLM proxy, verifies model aliases are registered, runs tiny completion probes. Catches auth failures early instead of failing mid-pipeline.

### Modified core modules

**`core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py`** — Two changes:
1. *Lazy imports* — avoids circular import issues by deferring heavy imports to call time
2. *Driver-ref short-circuit* — if a driver already has grounded PDF refs from extraction, skip the retrieval call entirely and use those refs as sources. Saves LLM calls.

**`core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`** — Three changes:
1. *Hardcoded system prompt* instead of loading from prompt store (the prompt file doesn't exist on dev yet)
2. *Proxy-aware cache control* — skips `cache_control` on system messages when behind a LiteLLM proxy (proxy doesn't support ephemeral caching)
3. *Uses `LLMRuntime.complete_structured()`* instead of raw API calls — routes through the shared proxy-safe runtime

**`core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py`** — The biggest change. Three things:
1. *Driver-scoped indicator identity* — indicator "GDP Growth" under Driver A and indicator "GDP Growth" under Driver B become two separate graph nodes, each tagged with their parent driver. Previously they'd collapse into one node.
2. *REFERENCE edge creation* — when an indicator is extracted from a source document, a REFERENCE edge is created from the information node to the indicator node. This gives indicators a provenance trail back to their sources.
3. *Driver-ID matching* — matches drivers by their graph node ID first (reliable), falling back to title/query matching (fuzzy). Previously only had fuzzy matching.

### Modified shared files

**`config/llm_tasks.toml`** — One line: `FS3.indicator_extraction` tier `large` → `medium`. The indicator extraction task uses a medium-tier model on dev_sprint, not large.

**`services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py`** — Four surgical changes:
1. Added `_has_indicator_literature_sources()` — checks if a driver has PDF refs or source contexts
2. Added `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers()` — filters the reduced driver list to only those that were actually admitted to the graph AND have literature sources. This is the "graph gate repair" — previously all reduced drivers went to indicator literature, even ones the graph rejected.
3. Added `"graph_delta"` key to `drivers_payload` so the filter function can see which drivers were admitted
4. Changed the Step 4 call site from `[x for x in reduced_drivers]` to `_graph_admitted_reduced_drivers(drivers_payload)`
5. Added `run_id` and `model_set` params to the main function signature (the live test passes these)

**`packages/inference/llm/__init__.py`** — Re-exports `run_litellm_preflight`, `LiteLLMPreflightError`, `ProviderAuthError` from the new preflight module.

### New tests

**`tests/test_graph_entity_validation.py`** — 8 unit tests for the validator: canonical endpoints, information→target rejection, question nodes, duplicate targets, shared indicators, driver→target fanout completeness.

**`tests/test_graph_entity_conformance.py`** — 4 cross-layer tests: verifies DB enums match the validator's canonical set, checks the golden graph fixture for known drift, confirms the conformance report doc tracks current blockers (repository coercion, stale JSON schema, legacy UI labels).

**`tests/test_live_graph_entity_process.py`** — 1 unit test (always runs) + 1 gated live test (requires env var). The unit test validates run-scoped graph rows. The live test runs the full pipeline on a seeded question and validates the returned + persisted graph.

**`tests/test_litellm_preflight.py`** — Unit tests for the preflight module (proxy readiness, model probes, auth failure detection).

### Modified tests

**`tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py`** — New tests for driver-scoped indicators, REFERENCE edge creation, driver-ID matching precedence, revised stage outcome logic. Existing test expectations updated (e.g., same indicator name now creates 2 nodes instead of 1).

**`tests/test_driver_indicator_literature.py`** — New test for the driver-ref short-circuit (when driver has PDF refs, skip retrieval entirely).

### Ported docs

**`docs/plans/definitions/graph-entity-definitions.md`** — Canonical definitions for all graph entity types and edge semantics. Referenced by the conformance tests.

**`docs/plans/definitions/graph-entity-conformance-report.md`** — Cross-layer conformance status (validator, DB, repositories, API, JSON schema, UI). Referenced by the conformance tests.

> AGENT

These are the rules for what a valid graph looks like. Think of them as the schema for the graph structure.

**`CANONICAL_NODE_TYPES`** — The only four types of node allowed in a graph:
- **target** — the thing being forecasted (e.g., "Will oil hit $100?"). Each option (Yes/No) gets a target node.
- **driver** — a causal factor that moves probability (e.g., "OPEC production cuts")
- **indicator** — a concrete, observable data point that evidences a driver (e.g., "Brent crude spot price is $87.40 as of March 2024")
- **information** — a source document the indicator/driver was extracted from (e.g., a Reuters article)

**`LEGACY_NODE_TYPE_ALIASES`** — Old names from before the vocabulary was standardized. `option` is now `target`, `evidence` is now `information`. The validator maps these so old data still works.

**`VALID_CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINTS`** — Which node types can be connected by a CONDITIONAL edge (meaning "this thing causally influences that thing"):
- indicator → driver (data point influences a causal factor)
- indicator → target (data point directly influences the forecast)
- driver → driver (one causal factor influences another)
- driver → target (causal factor influences the forecast)

Notice it's always "lower in the chain → higher in the chain." Information nodes never have CONDITIONAL edges — they're sources, not causes.

**`VALID_REFERENCE_ENDPOINTS`** — Which node types can be connected by a REFERENCE edge (meaning "this is where that came from" — provenance, not causation):
- information → indicator (this source document backs this data point)
- information → driver (this source document backs this causal factor)

Notably missing: information → target and driver → target REFERENCE edges are **invalid**. Targets don't need provenance links — they're defined by the question itself.

**`VALID_REFERENCE_KINDS`** — REFERENCE edges have a subtype:
- `source` — "this information node is the primary source for this indicator/driver"
- `evidence` — "this information node provides supporting evidence"

The DB also has `historic_data` as a reference kind, but the validator considers it non-canonical — that's one of the conformance blockers flagged in the report.

**`VALID_GRAPH_KINDS`** — Three modes the validator can run in:
- `generic` — basic checks only
- `driver_indicator` — validates just the indicator↔driver subgraph (used for the indicator extraction stage output)
- `full` — validates the complete graph including driver→target fanout completeness (every driver must connect to all targets, not just some)

```json
{
  "title": "Remove legacy graph node type aliases and enforce canonical types at all boundaries",
  "teamId": "6fc98d18-8a80-4c49-8c42-a6e2a4d3d722",
  "assigneeId": "c7a8f74f-a7ba-41e8-b771-85fc97b42216",
  "projectId": "cf9fb1a1-0344-45e5-933a-a689a00d3858",
  "priority": "3",
  "description": "## What this is\n\nThe graph validator currently aliases legacy node types (`option` → `target`, `evidence` → `information`) and the repository layer silently coerces unknown types (unknown nodes default to `driver`, unknown edges default to `REFERENCE`). This is tech debt that shouldn't exist in a new product.\n\n## Scope\n\n1. **Remove `LEGACY_NODE_TYPE_ALIASES`** from `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py` — stop accepting `option` and `evidence` as valid node types\n2. **Reject unknown types in `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py`** — `_node_type_for_record` should raise on unknown types instead of defaulting to `driver`; `_edge_record_to_row` should raise instead of defaulting to `REFERENCE`\n3. **Remove `historic_data` from `ReferenceKind` enum** in `packages/storage/database.py` if no code path produces it, or document why it exists\n4. **Update `core/schemas/graph.schema.json`** — remove stale types (`question`, `evidence`, `evidence_price_data`, `historic_event_set`, `INSTANTIATES`)\n5. **Update frontend legacy labels** in `frontend/theme.js` and `frontend/cytoscape-styles.js` — remove `evidence`/`option` compatibility paths\n6. **Update golden fixtures** (`tests/golden/graph_bundle.json`) if they use legacy types\n7. **Update the conformance report** to reflect resolved blockers\n\n## Why\n\nThis is a new product. Legacy aliases and silent coercion mask bugs instead of surfacing them. Every boundary should reject non-canonical types loudly so issues are caught at the source, not papered over downstream."
}
```

> AGENT

Created **[SPI-248](https://linear.app/spire-elijah/issue/SPI-248/remove-legacy-graph-node-type-aliases-and-enforce-canonical-types-at)** — "Remove legacy graph node type aliases and enforce canonical types at all boundaries", assigned to you, Medium priority, in Backlog.

> DEVELOPER

we have this comment:     """Validate graph records against canonical graph entity definitions.

    The validator is intentionally opt-in so legacy fixtures can be repaired
    before these checks become a global write barrier.
    """ explain it to me

> AGENT

That docstring is saying the validator is a read-only checker you call explicitly — it doesn't automatically block anything from being written to the database.

Right now the flow is:
1. Pipeline builds a graph
2. Graph gets persisted to DB
3. *Then* you can call `validate_graph_entities()` on the result to check if it's clean

What it's **not** doing is sitting at the DB write path rejecting bad data. The repository layer still silently coerces unknown types (the `_NODE_TYPE_MAP.get(raw_type, NodeType.DRIVER)` defaulting you saw).

The "legacy fixtures can be repaired" part is talking about things like `tests/golden/graph_bundle.json` which still uses old types like `option` and has `driver→target REFERENCE` edges that the validator flags. If the validator were a hard gate on writes, those fixtures (and any old data) would break immediately.

SPI-248 (the ticket you just created) is essentially the work to finish that transition — remove the aliases, make the repository reject instead of coerce, fix the fixtures, and turn the validator into that global write barrier.

> DEVELOPER

The repository layer (`sql_repositories.py`) still silently coerces unknown types — unknown nodes default to `driver`, unknown edges default to `REFERENCE`.\n\nThis ticket should finish that transition:\n1. Fix all legacy fixtures and data paths so they produce canonical types\n2. Make the repository reject non-canonical types instead of coercing\n3. Move the validator to the DB write path as a hard gate\n4. Remove the \"opt-in\" caveat from the docstring"
```json
}
```

> AGENT

So on dev, these are normal top-level imports and they work fine. The dev_sprint version replaced them with global-mutable-state lazy loading using `global` variables.

This is a bad pattern. It:
- Uses mutable module-level globals (`run_agent_single_scope_retrieval: Any | None = None`)
- Mutates them at call time via `global` keyword
- Throws away type information (everything is `Any`)
- Adds complexity for a problem that doesn't actually exist on dev — the imports work fine as top-level imports today

If there *were* a real circular import, the right fix would be to restructure the dependency, not paper over it with lazy globals.

Let me revert this file to use dev's normal imports while keeping the other meaningful changes (driver-ref short-circuit, submit_with_context).

```
72	        from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.config import LiteratureQueryConfig as _query_config_cls
73	
74	        EvidenceDownloadConfig = _download_config_cls
75	        LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig = _retrieval_config_cls
76	        LiteratureQueryConfig = _query_config_cls
77	    return (
78	        LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent,
79	        LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig,
80	        LiteratureQueryConfig,
81	        EvidenceDownloadConfig,
82	    )
83	
84	
85	def _support_rank(value: object) -> int:
86	    normalized = str(value or "").strip().lower()
87	    if normalized == "high":
88	        return 2
89	    if normalized == "medium":
90	        return 1
91	    return 0
92	
93	
94	def _is_fallback_driver(driver: Mapping[str, Any]) -> bool:
95	    return _shared_is_fallback_driver(driver)
96	
97	
98	def prioritize_drivers_for_indicator_literature(
99	    reduced_drivers: Sequence[dict[str, Any]],
100	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
101	    ranked: list[tuple[tuple[int, int, int, int], dict[str, Any]]] = []
102	    for index, driver in enumerate(reduced_drivers):
103	        if not isinstance(driver, dict):
104	            continue
105	        pdf_refs = driver.get("pdf_refs") if isinstance(driver.get("pdf_refs"), list) else []
106	        evidence_refs = len([ref for ref in pdf_refs if str(ref).strip()])
107	        ranked.append(
108	            (
109	                (
110	                    1 if evidence_refs > 0 else 0,
111	                    0 if _is_fallback_driver(driver) else 1,
112	                    _support_rank(driver.get("support_level")),
113	                    -index,
114	                ),
115	                driver,
116	            )
117	        )
118	    ranked.sort(key=lambda item: item[0], reverse=True)
119	    return [driver for _, driver in ranked]
120	
121	
122	def driver_scope_to_agent_scope(driver: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str:
123	    """
124	    Map a reduced-driver scope into Agent01's supported scope values.
125	    """
126	    retrieval_scope = str(driver.get("retrieval_scope") or "").strip().lower()
127	    if retrieval_scope in {"company_specific", "industry_specific", "market_wide", "cross_cutting_shock"}:
128	        return retrieval_scope
129	
130	    scope_raw = str(driver.get("scope") or "").strip().lower()
131	    if scope_raw in {"company_specific", "target_specific", "firm"}:
132	        return "company_specific"
133	    if scope_raw in {"industry_specific", "sector"}:
134	        return "industry_specific"
135	    if scope_raw in {"market_wide", "macro"}:
136	        return "market_wide"
137	    if scope_raw == "cross_cutting_shock":
138	        return "cross_cutting_shock"
139	
140	    ontology = driver.get("ontology") if isinstance(driver.get("ontology"), dict) else {}
141	    ont_scope = str((ontology or {}).get("scope") or "").strip().lower()
142	    if ont_scope == "firm":
143	        return "company_specific"
144	    if ont_scope == "sector":
145	        return "industry_specific"
146	    if ont_scope == "macro":
147	        return "market_wide"
148	
149	    return "market_wide"
150	
151	
152	def build_driver_indicator_query(driver: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str:
153	    """
154	    Build a retrieval query for indicator evidence around a reduced driver.
155	    """
156	    search_query = str(driver.get("search_query") or "").strip()
157	    title = str(driver.get("title") or "").strip()
158	    description = str(driver.get("description") or "").strip()
159	    market_channel = str(driver.get("market_channel") or "").strip()
160	    mechanism = str(driver.get("mechanism") or "").strip()
161	    evidence_basis = str(driver.get("evidence_basis") or "").strip()
162	
163	    base = search_query or title or mechanism or description or evidence_basis or "driver evidence"
164	    if market_channel and market_channel != "other":
165	        base = f"{base} {market_channel}"
166	    indicator_terms = "indicator signal proxy measure confirmation disconfirmation"
167	    return f"{base} {indicator_terms}".strip()
168	
169	
170	def _safe_driver_slug(title: str, index: int) -> str:
171	    normalized = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "_", str(title or "").lower()).strip("_")
172	    if not normalized:
173	        normalized = f"driver_{index:03d}"
174	    return normalized[:80]
175	
176	
177	def _selected_sources_from_driver_refs(driver: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
178	    """Carry grounded driver evidence titles into indicator extraction when per-driver retrieval is empty."""
179	    selected: list[dict[str, str]] = []
180	    seen: set[str] = set()
181	
182	    def _append_source(*, title: str, source_id: str, source_mode: str, **extra: str) -> None:
183	        clean_title = str(title or "").strip()
184	        clean_source_id = str(source_id or clean_title).strip()
185	        key = clean_source_id or clean_title
186	        if not key or key in seen:
187	            return
188	        seen.add(key)
189	        row = {
190	            "title": clean_title or clean_source_id,
191	            "source_id": clean_source_id or clean_title,
192	            "source_mode": source_mode,
193	        }
194	        for extra_key, extra_value in extra.items():
195	            clean_value = str(extra_value or "").strip()
196	            if clean_value:
197	                row[extra_key] = clean_value
198	        selected.append(row)
199	
200	    for raw_ref in driver.get("pdf_refs") or []:
201	        title = str(raw_ref or "").strip()
202	        _append_source(
203	            title=title,
204	            source_id=title,
205	            source_mode="driver_extraction_pdf_ref",
206	        )
207	    source_contexts = driver.get("source_contexts") if isinstance(driver.get("source_contexts"), list) else []
208	    for context in source_contexts:
209	        if not isinstance(context, Mapping):
210	            continue
211	        title = str(context.get("document_title") or context.get("pdf_ref") or context.get("pdf_file") or "").strip()
212	        source_id = str(
213	            context.get("evidence_node_id") or context.get("document_key") or context.get("pdf_file") or context.get("pdf_ref") or title
214	        ).strip()
215	        _append_source(
216	            title=title,
217	            source_id=source_id,
218	            source_mode="driver_extraction_source_context",
219	            pdf_filename=str(context.get("pdf_file") or "").strip(),
220	            entry_id=str(context.get("evidence_node_id") or "").strip(),
221	        )
222	    return selected
223	
224	
225	def _driver_indicator_result_row(
226	    *,
227	    index: int,
228	    driver: Mapping[str, Any],
229	    scope_used: str,
230	    indicator_query: str,
231	    status: str,
232	    error: str = "",
233	    candidate_count: int = 0,
234	    downloaded_files: Sequence[str] | None = None,
235	    selected_sources: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]] | None = None,
236	    local_docs_fallback_used: bool = False,
237	    driver_dir: Path | None = None,
238	    retrieval_attempted: bool,
239	    placeholder_reason: str = "",
240	) -> dict[str, Any]:
241	    title = str(driver.get("title") or "").strip() or f"Driver {index}"
242	    downloaded_list = [str(path) for path in (downloaded_files or []) if str(path).strip()]
243	    selected_sources_list = [dict(item) for item in (selected_sources or []) if isinstance(item, Mapping)]
244	    return {
245	        "driver_index": int(index),
246	        "driver_id": str(driver.get("driver_id") or "").strip(),
247	        "driver_label": title,
248	        "driver_title": title,
249	        "driver_search_query": str(driver.get("search_query") or "").strip(),
250	        "indicator_query": indicator_query,
251	        "scope_used": scope_used,
252	        "status": str(status or "").strip() or "ok",
253	        "error": str(error or "").strip(),
254	        "candidate_count": int(candidate_count),
255	        "downloaded_count": len(downloaded_list),
256	        "downloaded_files": downloaded_list,
257	        "pdfs": downloaded_list,
258	        "selected_sources": selected_sources_list,
259	        "items": selected_sources_list,
260	        "local_docs_fallback_used": bool(local_docs_fallback_used),
261	        "driver_dir": str(driver_dir) if driver_dir is not None else "",
262	        "retrieval_attempted": bool(retrieval_attempted),
263	        "placeholder_reason": str(placeholder_reason or "").strip(),
264	    }
265
```
