267	@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
268	class DriverIndicatorLiteratureConfig:
269	    enabled: bool = True
270	    pdfs_per_driver: int = 2
271	    feedthrough: int = 2
272	    max_drivers: int | None = None
273	    domain_profile_model: str | None = None
274	    local_repo_path: Path | None = None
275	    prefer_local_repo: bool = False
276	
277	
278	def run_driver_indicator_literature_for_question(
279	    *,
280	    question_id: str,
281	    graphs_dir: Path,
282	    graph_id: str,
283	    question_dir: Path,
284	    toi: datetime,
285	    reduced_drivers: Sequence[dict[str, Any]],
286	    config: DriverIndicatorLiteratureConfig,
287	    storage_adapters: WorkerStorageAdapters | None = None,
288	    graph_store_paths: GraphStorePaths | None = None,
289	) -> dict[str, Any]:
290	    """
291	    Run Agent01 retrieval per reduced driver and write per-driver result files.
292	    """
293	    base_payload: dict[str, Any] = {
294	        "generated_at": _now_iso(),
295	        "question_id": str(question_id or "").strip(),
296	        "enabled": bool(config.enabled),
297	        "config": {
298	            "pdfs_per_driver": int(config.pdfs_per_driver),
299	            "feedthrough": int(config.feedthrough),
300	            "max_drivers": int(config.max_drivers) if config.max_drivers is not None else None,
301	            "domain_profile_model": str(config.domain_profile_model or "").strip(),
302	            "local_repo_path": str(config.local_repo_path) if config.local_repo_path is not None else "",
303	            "prefer_local_repo": bool(config.prefer_local_repo),
304	        },
305	        "counts": {
306	            "driver_total": len([x for x in reduced_drivers if isinstance(x, dict)]),
307	            "driver_processed": 0,
308	            "driver_ok": 0,
309	            "driver_failed": 0,
310	            "driver_placeholder": 0,
311	            "pdf_count": 0,
312	        },
313	        "drivers": [],
314	        "results": [],
315	    }
316	
317	    resolved_storage = resolve_worker_storage_adapters(storage_adapters)
318	    resolved_graph_store_paths = graph_store_paths or resolve_graph_store_paths(graphs_dir=graphs_dir)
319	
320	    driver_root = question_dir / "driver_indicators"
321	    driver_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
322	
323	    max_drivers = int(config.max_drivers) if config.max_drivers is not None else None
324	    all_drivers = [x for x in reduced_drivers if isinstance(x, dict)]
325	    prioritized_drivers = prioritize_drivers_for_indicator_literature(all_drivers)
326	    retrieval_driver_ids: set[int] = set()
327	    if bool(config.enabled) and int(config.pdfs_per_driver) > 0:
328	        retrieval_targets = prioritized_drivers
329	        if max_drivers is not None:
330	            retrieval_targets = retrieval_targets[: max(0, max_drivers)]
331	        retrieval_driver_ids = {id(driver) for driver in retrieval_targets}
332	
333	    results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
334	    total_pdf_count = 0
335	    ok_count = 0
336	    failed_count = 0
337	
338	    placeholder_count = 0
339	
340	    def _process_single_driver(idx: int, driver: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
341	        indicator_query = build_driver_indicator_query(driver)
342	        scope_used = driver_scope_to_agent_scope(driver)
343	
344	        if not bool(config.enabled):
345	            return _driver_indicator_result_row(
346	                index=idx,
347	                driver=driver,
348	                scope_used=scope_used,
349	                indicator_query=indicator_query,
350	                status="indicator_retrieval_disabled",
351	                retrieval_attempted=False,
352	                placeholder_reason="indicator_retrieval_disabled",
353	            )
354	        if int(config.pdfs_per_driver) <= 0:
355	            return _driver_indicator_result_row(
356	                index=idx,
357	                driver=driver,
358	                scope_used=scope_used,
359	                indicator_query=indicator_query,
360	                status="indicator_retrieval_budget_zero",
361	                retrieval_attempted=False,
362	                placeholder_reason="indicator_retrieval_budget_zero",
363	            )
364	        if id(driver) not in retrieval_driver_ids:
365	            return _driver_indicator_result_row(
366	                index=idx,
367	                driver=driver,
368	                scope_used=scope_used,
369	                indicator_query=indicator_query,
370	                status="indicator_retrieval_skipped_budget",
371	                retrieval_attempted=False,
372	                placeholder_reason="indicator_retrieval_skipped_budget",
373	            )
374	
375	        title = str(driver.get("title") or "").strip() or f"Driver {idx}"
376	        driver_dir = driver_root / f"{idx:03d}_{_safe_driver_slug(title, idx)}"
377	        evidence_dir = driver_dir / "evidence"
378	        driver_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
379	        evidence_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
380	
381	        status = "ok"
382	        error = ""
383	        downloaded_files: list[str] = []
384	        scope_detail: dict[str, Any] = {}
385	        local_docs_fallback_used = False
386	        selected_sources: list[dict[str, str]] = []
387	
388	        try:
389	            selected_sources = _selected_sources_from_driver_refs(driver)
390	            if selected_sources:
391	                result_row = _driver_indicator_result_row(
392	                    index=idx,
393	                    driver=driver,
394	                    scope_used=scope_used,
395	                    indicator_query=indicator_query,
396	                    status=status,
397	                    candidate_count=len(selected_sources),
398	                    downloaded_files=[],
399	                    selected_sources=selected_sources,
400	                    local_docs_fallback_used=False,
401	                    driver_dir=driver_dir,
402	                    retrieval_attempted=True,
403	                )
404	                (driver_dir / "result.json").write_text(json.dumps(result_row, indent=2, ensure_ascii=True), encoding="utf-8")
405	                return result_row
406	
407	            graph = BayesianGraph(
408	                question_id=str(question_id or "").strip(),
409	                graphs_dir=graphs_dir,
410	                graph_id=str(graph_id or "").strip() or DEFAULT_GRAPH_ID,
411	                expert_lit_dir=str(evidence_dir),
412	                graph_store_paths=resolved_graph_store_paths,
413	                graph_store_repository=resolved_storage.graph_store_repository,
414	                horizon={"value": 1, "unit": "month"},
415	                market="Global Market",
416	            )
417	            graph.outcome_description = title
418	            (
419	                retrieval_agent_cls,
420	                retrieval_config_cls,
421	                query_config_cls,
422	                download_config_cls,
423	            ) = _get_retrieval_components()
424	            cfg = retrieval_config_cls(
425	                query=query_config_cls(use_llm_query_boost=False),
426	                download=download_config_cls(
427	                    default_pdfs_per_scope=int(config.pdfs_per_driver),
428	                    default_feedthrough=int(config.feedthrough),
429	                    auto_select_all=True,
430	                ),
431	            )
432	            agent_kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {"graph": graph, "config": cfg}
433	            if config.local_repo_path is not None:
434	                agent_kwargs["local_docs_root"] = config.local_repo_path
435	            domain_profile_model = str(config.domain_profile_model or "").strip()
436	            if domain_profile_model:
437	                agent_kwargs["domain_profile_model"] = domain_profile_model
438	            agent = retrieval_agent_cls(**agent_kwargs)
439	            retrieval_runner = _get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval()
440	            retrieval = retrieval_runner(
441	                agent=agent,
442	                toi=toi,
443	                scope=scope_used,
444	                query=indicator_query,
445	                pdfs_per_scope=int(config.pdfs_per_driver),
446	                feedthrough=int(config.feedthrough),
447	                clear_first=False,
448	                add_evidence_nodes=False,
449	                prefer_local_repo=bool(config.prefer_local_repo),
450	            )
451	            downloaded_files = list(retrieval.downloaded_files)
452	            scope_detail = dict(retrieval.scope_detail)
453	            selected_sources = list(retrieval.selected_sources)
454	            local_docs_fallback_used = bool(retrieval.local_docs_fallback_used)
455	            if not selected_sources:
456	                selected_sources = _selected_sources_from_driver_refs(driver)
457	                if selected_sources:
458	                    scope_detail["candidate_count"] = max(int(scope_detail.get("candidate_count") or 0), len(selected_sources))
459	        except Exception as exc:
460	            status = "failed"
461	            error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
462	
463	        result_row = _driver_indicator_result_row(
464	            index=idx,
465	            driver=driver,
466	            scope_used=scope_used,
467	            indicator_query=indicator_query,
468	            status=status,
469	            error=error,
470	            candidate_count=int(scope_detail.get("candidate_count") or 0),
471	            downloaded_files=downloaded_files,
472	            selected_sources=selected_sources,
473	            local_docs_fallback_used=bool(local_docs_fallback_used),
474	            driver_dir=driver_dir,
475	            retrieval_attempted=True,
476	        )
477	        (driver_dir / "result.json").write_text(json.dumps(result_row, indent=2, ensure_ascii=True), encoding="utf-8")
478	        return result_row
479	
480	    import concurrent.futures
481	
482	    with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=3) as executor:
483	        futures = {
484	            submit_with_context(executor, _process_single_driver, idx, driver): idx for idx, driver in enumerate(all_drivers, start=1)
485	        }
486	        for future in concurrent.futures.as_completed(futures):
487	            result_row = future.result()
488	            results.append(result_row)
489	            row_status = str(result_row.get("status") or "").strip()
490	            if not result_row.get("retrieval_attempted"):
491	                placeholder_count += 1
492	            elif row_status == "ok":
493	                ok_count += 1
494	                total_pdf_count += len(result_row.get("downloaded_files") or [])
495	            else:
496	                failed_count += 1
497	
498	    payload = dict(base_payload)
499	    payload["counts"] = {
500	        "driver_total": len(all_drivers),
501	        "driver_processed": len(results),
502	        "driver_ok": int(ok_count),
503	        "driver_failed": int(failed_count),
504	        "driver_placeholder": int(placeholder_count),
505	        "pdf_count": int(total_pdf_count),
506	    }
507	    payload["drivers"] = results
508	    payload["results"] = results
509	    return payload
510

Well, I was wrong — the circular import is real. The chain is:

```
driver_indicator_literature
  → literature_retrieval_runtime
    → research_driver_shared
      → services.worker (init)
        → research_driver_batch_pipeline
          → research_driver_question_pipeline
            → research_driver_shared  ← circular
```

The lazy imports exist to break this cycle. It's not a *good* pattern — the right fix is to break the circular dependency by restructuring the imports — but it's there for a real reason, not for nothing.

Let me revert and we can track the proper fix separately.

  "description": "## What this is\n\n`core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py` uses lazy imports with mutable module-level globals to work around a circular import. This is bad — it throws away type information (everything becomes `Any`), uses `global` mutation at call time, and hides the real problem.\n\n## The circular import chain\n\n```\ndriver_indicator_literature\n  → literature_retrieval_runtime\n    → research_driver_shared\n      → services.worker.__init__\n        → research_driver_batch_pipeline\n          → research_driver_question_pipeline\n            → research_driver_shared  ← circular\n```\n\n## Why it matters\n\n- All lazy-imported symbols lose their types (declared as `Any | None`)\n- Mutable globals make the module harder to reason about\n- The real fix is to restructure the dependency so the cycle doesn't exist\n\n## Likely fix\n\nThe root cause is `services/worker/__init__.py` re-exporting from `research_driver_batch_pipeline`, which pulls in the entire pipeline chain at import time. Options:\n1. Stop re-exporting pipeline internals from `services.worker.__init__`\n2. Move `research_driver_shared` utilities out of the cycle (e.g., into `packages` or a standalone module)\n3. Make `research_driver_batch_pipeline` import `research_driver_question_pipeline` lazily at call sites instead of at module level\n\nOnce the cycle is broken, restore normal top-level imports in `driver_indicator_literature.py` and remove the `_get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval()` / `_get_retrieval_components()` lazy-loading machinery."
}

The root cause is likely `services/worker/__init__.py` eagerly re-exporting pipeline internals, which drags the whole chain in at import time.

what is this pattern??

    global LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent
    global LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig
    global LiteratureQueryConfig

        )

        )
        from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.config import LiteratureQueryConfig as _query_config_cls

> AGENT

It's a hand-rolled lazy singleton loader. On first call it imports the real classes, stashes them in module globals, and on subsequent calls returns the cached references. Basically reimplementing what `importlib` already does, but worse.

Problems:
- **Types are gone** — the globals are declared `Any | None`, so every downstream usage is untyped
- **Mutable module state** — `global` keyword mutating module-level variables at runtime
- **Returns a tuple of `Any`** — the caller has to destructure into meaningless positional variables (`retrieval_agent_cls`, `retrieval_config_cls`, etc.) with no type checking
- **The `if None` guards are redundant** — Python's import system already caches modules after first import. A bare `from X import Y` inside the function body would achieve the same lazy-load effect without any globals

If you *had* to defer the import (which we do here because of the circular dependency), this is all you need:

```python
def _run_single_driver(...):
    from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.agent import LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent
    from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.config import (
        EvidenceDownloadConfig,
        LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig,
        LiteratureQueryConfig,
    )
    # use them directly
```

Python caches the import after the first call. No globals, no `None` sentinels, no tuple unpacking, and the type checker can see the real types. SPI-249 should clean this up when the circular import is fixed (or even before — just moving the imports into the function body is strictly better than the current pattern).

```json
{
  "id": "SPI-249",
  "description": "## What this is\n\n`core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py` uses lazy imports with mutable module-level globals to work around a circular import. This is bad — it throws away type information (everything becomes `Any`), uses `global` mutation at call time, and hides the real problem.\n\n## The circular import chain\n\n```\ndriver_indicator_literature\n  → literature_retrieval_runtime\n    → research_driver_shared\n      → services.worker.__init__\n        → research_driver_batch_pipeline\n          → research_driver_question_pipeline\n            → research_driver_shared  ← circular\n```\n\n## Why it matters\n\n- All lazy-imported symbols lose their types (declared as `Any | None`)\n- Mutable globals make the module harder to reason about\n- The real fix is to restructure the dependency so the cycle doesn't exist\n\n## The current pattern is over-engineered\n\nThe `_get_retrieval_components()` function is a hand-rolled lazy singleton loader: it checks if module globals are `None`, imports the real classes, stashes them in globals, and returns a `tuple[Any, Any, Any, Any]` that the caller destructures into meaningless positional variables.\n\nThis is unnecessary. Python's import system already caches modules after first import. If you need to defer an import to avoid a circular dependency, a bare `from X import Y` inside the function body does the same thing — no globals, no `None` sentinels, no tuple unpacking, and the type checker can see the real types:\n\n```python\ndef _run_single_driver(...):\n    from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.agent import LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent\n    from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.config import (\n        EvidenceDownloadConfig,\n        LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig,\n        LiteratureQueryConfig,\n    )\n    # use them directly with full type information\n```\n\n## Two-phase fix\n\n**Phase 1 (immediate, no architectural change):** Replace the `_get_retrieval_components()` / `_get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval()` global-mutation machinery with simple function-scoped lazy imports. Same effect, no globals, types preserved.\n\n**Phase 2 (break the cycle):** The root cause is `services/worker/__init__.py` re-exporting from `research_driver_batch_pipeline`, which pulls in the entire pipeline chain at import time. Options:\n1. Stop re-exporting pipeline internals from `services.worker.__init__`\n2. Move `research_driver_shared` utilities out of the cycle (e.g., into `packages` or a standalone module)\n3. Make `research_driver_batch_pipeline` import `research_driver_question_pipeline` lazily at call sites instead of at module level\n\nOnce the cycle is broken, restore normal top-level imports in `driver_indicator_literature.py` and remove the lazy imports entirely."
}

1	 ## Code quality patterns
2	
3	  ### Anti-patterns — reject on sight
4	
5	  - **Inline system prompts** — prompts must live in `prompts/` and load via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`.
6	  Never `_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "..."`.
7	  - **JSON files as storage** — all persistent state goes through the database via `packages/storage/`. JSON file
8	  storage is retired.
9	  - **Defensive string wrapping on comparisons** — `str(x or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"` adds nothing over
10	   `x == "CONDITIONAL"`. Reject it.
11	  - **Global mutation + lazy imports** — `global _x` inside a function body with an inline import is a scratch
12	  pattern. Revert it.
13	  - **Dropped model resolution fallback** — use `model or resolve_role_model("tool")`, not `str(model or
14	  "").strip()`.
15	
16	  ### Architectural patterns — always follow
17	
18	  - LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool` and every `LLMConfig()` must have `task_id=`.
19	  - Use `resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` for named tasks, `resolve_role_model("tool"|"agent")` for generic
20	  fallbacks. Never hardcode model names.
21	  - All stage results use `build_stage_outcome()`. Never return ad-hoc dicts.
22	  - All graph nodes use `build_node_data()` / `build_origin()` / `build_provenance()`.
23	  - Direct SQL access is confined to `packages/storage/database.py`. Domain code never touches S
24	
```

> AGENT

One test imports it to assert that the tool resolves to the right model. That's it.

The test could just call `resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")` directly. The module-level constant is:
- Resolving a model name at import time (side effect)
- Exporting it as a public constant for one test assertion
- Labeled "compatibility alias for legacy importers" when there are no legacy importers — just one test

It's not harmful, but it's unnecessary. The test should own its own assertion value. Want me to clean it up or leave it for now?

```
1	 ## Code quality patterns
2	
3	  ### Anti-patterns — reject on sight
4	
5	  - **Inline system prompts** — prompts must live in `prompts/` and load via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`.
6	  Never `_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "..."`.
7	  - **JSON files as storage** — all persistent state goes through the database via `packages/storage/`. JSON file
8	  storage is retired.
9	  - **Defensive string wrapping on comparisons** — `str(x or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"` adds nothing over
10	   `x == "CONDITIONAL"`. Reject it.
11	  - **Global mutation + lazy imports** — `global _x` inside a function body with an inline import is a scratch
12	  pattern. Revert it. If a circular import forces deferred loading, use a function-scoped `from X import Y`
13	  (Python caches imports after first call). Never use module-level `Any | None` sentinels with `global`
14	  mutation and tuple-of-`Any` return values — it destroys type information for no benefit.
15	  - **Dropped model resolution fallback** — use `model or resolve_role_model("tool")`, not `str(model or
16	  "").strip()`.
17	
18	  ### Architectural patterns — always follow
19	
20	  - LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool` and every `LLMConfig()` must have `task_id=`.
21	  - Use `resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` for named tasks, `resolve_role_model("tool"|"agent")` for generic
22	  fallbacks. Never hardcode model names.
23	  - All stage results use `build_stage_outcome()`. Never return ad-hoc dicts.
24	  - All graph nodes use `build_node_data()` / `build_origin()` / `build_provenance()`.
25	  - Direct SQL access is confined to `packages/storage/database.py`. Domain code never touches S
26	
```

> DEVELOPER

wtf is this about?             {"role": "system", "content": system_prompt, "cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}},
            {
                "role": "system",
                "content": system_prompt,
                **({} if os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL") else {"cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}}),
            },

> AGENT

That's checking an environment variable at call time to decide whether to include `cache_control` on the system message. The idea is that the LiteLLM proxy doesn't support Anthropic's ephemeral caching, so when you're behind a proxy, skip it.

Two problems:

1. **Reading `os.environ` in domain code** — infrastructure concerns like "am I behind a proxy" shouldn't leak into extraction logic. The LLM runtime layer should handle this.

2. **The `LLMRuntime` already handles this** — the ported code also uses `self._runtime.complete_structured()` in the `DriverIndicatorExtractionTool` class, which routes through the runtime that already knows about proxy capabilities. This `cache_control` conditional is in the older `extract_causal_relationships()` function which calls the API directly.

Let me check if this function is even still called.

> DEVELOPER

what is going on/ Why is a markdown file being read bu tests?

> AGENT

The test is checking that the conformance report (a doc that tracks known cross-layer blockers) stays in sync with the actual code. It reads the markdown file and asserts things like:

- "If the golden graph fixture has a `driver_target_reference_edge` issue, the report must mention it"
- "If the DB enum has `HISTORIC_DATA`, the report must mention it"
- "If `sql_repositories.py` still silently coerces unknown types, the report must mention it"

It's basically a test that says "if these blockers still exist in the code, the doc must acknowledge them." The idea is to prevent the report from going stale — if someone fixes a blocker but doesn't update the report, or if a new blocker appears and the report doesn't mention it, the test fails.

Whether that's a good idea is a different question. It couples a test to the contents of a prose document, which means you can't edit the doc without thinking about the test and vice versa. And it's testing documentation accuracy, not code behavior.

Yeah, it's a pattern that exists elsewhere in the codebase:

- **`tests/test_fs10_validation_docs.py`** — reads `docs/guides/fs10_validation.md` and asserts it mentions specific CLI commands and file paths

1	"""LiteLLM provider preflight checks for live validation runs."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import os
6	from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
7	from typing import Any
8	
9	import httpx
10	
11	from .tiers import (
12	    LLMTier,
13	    load_llm_task_definitions,
14	    load_llm_tier_profiles,
15	    normalize_tier,
16	    resolve_model_set_name,
17	    resolved_expected_litellm_models,
18	)
19	
20	OPENROUTER_AUTH_FAILURE_MARKERS = (
21	    "no cookie auth credentials found",
22	    "openrouter",
23	)
24	
25	
26	class LiteLLMPreflightError(RuntimeError):
27	    """Base error for LiteLLM preflight failures."""
28	
29	
30	class ProviderAuthError(LiteLLMPreflightError):
31	    """Raised when a configured upstream provider rejects authentication."""
32	
33	
34	def _headers(*, api_key: str, master_key: str) -> dict[str, str]:
35	    key = api_key or master_key
36	    return {"Authorization": f"Bearer {key}"} if key else {}
37	
38	
39	def _response_text(response: httpx.Response) -> str:
40	    try:
41	        return response.text
42	    except Exception:
43	        return ""
44	
45	
46	def _is_provider_auth_failure(response: httpx.Response) -> bool:
47	    text = _response_text(response).lower()
48	    if response.status_code == 401:
49	        return True
50	    return all(marker in text for marker in OPENROUTER_AUTH_FAILURE_MARKERS)
51	
52	
53	def _available_model_names(payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> set[str]:
54	    out: set[str] = set()
55	    for entry in payload.get("data", []) if isinstance(payload.get("data"), list) else []:
56	        if not isinstance(entry, Mapping):
57	            continue
58	        model_name = str(entry.get("model_name") or entry.get("id") or "").strip()
59	        if model_name:
60	            out.add(model_name)
61	    return out
62	
63	
64	def _extract_completion_text(payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str:
65	    choices = payload.get("choices")
66	    if not isinstance(choices, list) or not choices:
67	        return ""
68	    first = choices[0]
69	    if not isinstance(first, Mapping):
70	        return ""
71	    message = first.get("message")
72	    if isinstance(message, Mapping):
73	        return str(message.get("content") or "").strip()
74	    return str(first.get("text") or "").strip()
75	
76	
77	def _normalize_task_ids(task_ids: Sequence[str] | None) -> tuple[str, ...]:
78	    out: list[str] = []
79	    seen: set[str] = set()
80	    for raw in task_ids or ():
81	        task_id = str(raw or "").strip()
82	        if not task_id or task_id in seen:
83	            continue
84	        seen.add(task_id)
85	        out.append(task_id)
86	    return tuple(out)
87	
88	
89	def _task_probe_specs(
90	    *,
91	    task_ids: tuple[str, ...],
92	    tier_models: Mapping[LLMTier, str],
93	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
94	    tasks = load_llm_task_definitions()
95	    by_model: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
96	    for task_id in task_ids:
97	        task = tasks.get(task_id)
98	        if task is None:
99	            available = ", ".join(sorted(tasks))
100	            raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"Unknown LLM task_id {task_id!r}; declared tasks: {available}")
101	        model = str(tier_models.get(task.tier) or "").strip()
102	        if not model:
103	            raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"model set does not define tier {task.tier.value!r} for task {task_id!r}")
104	        spec = by_model.setdefault(model, {"model": model, "tier": task.tier.value, "task_ids": []})
105	        spec["task_ids"].append(task_id)
106	    return list(by_model.values())
107	
108	
109	def run_litellm_preflight(
110	    *,
111	    model_set: str | None = None,
112	    probe_tier: str | LLMTier = LLMTier.MEDIUM,
113	    probe_task_ids: Sequence[str] | None = None,
114	    proxy_url: str | None = None,
115	    api_key: str | None = None,
116	    master_key: str | None = None,
117	    timeout_seconds: float = 15.0,
118	) -> dict[str, Any]:
119	    """Validate LiteLLM readiness, model aliases, and tiny provider completions."""
120	    resolved_proxy = str(proxy_url or os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL", "") or "").strip().rstrip("/")
121	    if not resolved_proxy:
122	        raise LiteLLMPreflightError("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL is required for live provider preflight")
123	
124	    resolved_model_set = resolve_model_set_name(model_set)
125	    tier = normalize_tier(probe_tier)
126	    task_ids = _normalize_task_ids(probe_task_ids)
127	    tier_models = load_llm_tier_profiles().get(resolved_model_set)
128	    if tier_models is None:
129	        available_sets = ", ".join(sorted(load_llm_tier_profiles()))
130	        raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"Unknown model_set {resolved_model_set!r}; available model sets: {available_sets}")
131	    if task_ids:
132	        probe_specs = _task_probe_specs(task_ids=task_ids, tier_models=tier_models)
133	        expected_models = frozenset(str(spec["model"]) for spec in probe_specs)
134	    else:
135	        probe_model = str(tier_models[tier]).strip()
136	        probe_specs = [{"model": probe_model, "tier": tier.value, "task_ids": []}]
137	        expected_models = resolved_expected_litellm_models(model_set=resolved_model_set)
138	    headers = _headers(
139	        api_key=str(api_key or os.environ.get("LITELLM_API_KEY", "") or "").strip(),
140	        master_key=str(master_key or os.environ.get("LITELLM_MASTER_KEY", "") or "").strip(),
141	    )
142	
143	    try:
144	        with httpx.Client(timeout=timeout_seconds) as client:
145	            readiness = client.get(f"{resolved_proxy}/health/readiness", headers=headers)
146	            readiness.raise_for_status()
147	
148	            model_info = client.get(f"{resolved_proxy}/model/info", headers=headers)
149	            model_info.raise_for_status()
150	            model_payload = model_info.json()
151	            if not isinstance(model_payload, Mapping):
152	                raise LiteLLMPreflightError("LiteLLM /model/info response was not a JSON object")
153	            available_models = _available_model_names(model_payload)
154	            missing_models = sorted(expected_models - available_models)
155	            if missing_models:
156	                raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM model aliases missing for {resolved_model_set}: {', '.join(missing_models)}")
157	
158	            completion_results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
159	            for spec in probe_specs:
160	                model = str(spec["model"])
161	                completion_payload = {
162	                    "model": model,
163	                    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Return exactly OK."}],
164	                    "temperature": 0,
165	                    "max_tokens": 4,
166	                }
167	                completion = client.post(f"{resolved_proxy}/chat/completions", headers=headers, json=completion_payload)
168	                if _is_provider_auth_failure(completion):
169	                    message = _response_text(completion).strip() or f"HTTP {completion.status_code}"
170	                    raise ProviderAuthError(f"provider auth failed for model {model}: {message}")
171	                try:
172	                    completion.raise_for_status()
173	                except httpx.HTTPStatusError as exc:
174	                    raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM completion probe failed for model {model}: {exc}") from exc
175	                completion_json = completion.json()
176	                if not isinstance(completion_json, Mapping):
177	                    raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM completion response was not a JSON object for model {model}")
178	                completion_text = _extract_completion_text(completion_json)
179	                if not completion_text:
180	                    raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM completion probe returned no content for model {model}")
181	                completion_results.append(
182	                    {
183	                        "model": model,
184	                        "tier": str(spec["tier"]),
185	                        "task_ids": list(spec.get("task_ids") or []),
186	                        "completion_text": completion_text,
187	                    }
188	                )
189	    except ProviderAuthError:
190	        raise
191	    except LiteLLMPreflightError:
192	        raise
193	    except httpx.HTTPStatusError as exc:
194	        response = exc.response
195	        if response is not None and _is_provider_auth_failure(response):
196	            message = _response_text(response).strip() or f"HTTP {response.status_code}"
197	            raise ProviderAuthError(f"provider auth failed: {message}") from exc
198	        raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM preflight HTTP failure: {exc}") from exc
199	    except httpx.HTTPError as exc:
200	        raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM preflight connection failure: {exc}") from exc
201	    except ValueError as exc:
202	        raise LiteLLMPreflightError(f"LiteLLM preflight response parsing failed: {exc}") from exc
203	
204	    return {

Three things jump out:

1. **`os.environ` deep in the function** (lines 120, 139, 140) — same anti-pattern we just flagged. The proxy URL, API key, and master key are all read from env vars inside the function body. These should be resolved at the call site and passed in. The function already accepts them as parameters (`proxy_url`, `api_key`, `master_key`) — the env var fallbacks should be the caller's responsibility, not buried here.

2. **Defensive `str(... or "").strip()` everywhere** — lines 120, 135, 139, 140, 160. That's the lookout.md anti-pattern. Most of these values already come from typed sources.

3. **Raw `httpx` calls instead of going through the LLM runtime** — this module builds its own HTTP client, constructs raw `/chat/completions` payloads, and parses raw JSON responses. It's a parallel LLM calling path that bypasses the `LLMRuntime` entirely. Arguably justified since it's a *preflight check* (you're testing whether the infra works before trusting the runtime), but it means any proxy quirks have to be handled in two places.

None of these are blockers for the PR — the module works and is well-tested. But the env var reads and defensive wrapping are worth cleaning up. Want me to make a ticket or roll it into SPI-250?

1	 ## Code quality patterns
2	
3	  ### Anti-patterns — reject on sight
4	
5	  - **Inline system prompts** — prompts must live in `prompts/` and load via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`.
6	  Never `_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "..."`.
7	  - **JSON files as storage** — all persistent state goes through the database via `packages/storage/`. JSON file
8	  storage is retired.
9	  - **Defensive string wrapping on comparisons** — `str(x or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"` adds nothing over
10	   `x == "CONDITIONAL"`. Reject it.
11	  - **Global mutation + lazy imports** — `global _x` inside a function body with an inline import is a scratch
12	  pattern. Revert it. If a circular import forces deferred loading, use a function-scoped `from X import Y`
13	  (Python caches imports after first call). Never use module-level `Any | None` sentinels with `global`
14	  mutation and tuple-of-`Any` return values — it destroys type information for no benefit.
15	  - **Dropped model resolution fallback** — use `model or resolve_role_model("tool")`, not `str(model or
16	  "").strip()`.
17	  - **Module-level model resolution constants** — `MY_MODEL = resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` at module scope
18	  resolves at import time and exports an internal detail as a public constant. Call `resolve_task_model()`
19	  at the point of use. Tests should call it directly, not import a "compatibility alias".
20	  - **`os.environ` deep in domain code** — environment variables should be read at the edges (config loading,
21	  CLI entry points, runtime initialization) and threaded through as typed config. Checking
22	  `os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL")` inside a function that builds LLM messages means behavior
23	  changes silently based on env state, is untestable without monkeypatching `os.environ`, and buries
24	  configuration decisions where no one will find them.
25	
26	  ### Architectural patterns — always follow
27	
28	  - LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool` and every `LLMConfig()` must have `task_id=`.
29	  - Use `resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` for named tasks, `resolve_role_model("tool"|"agent")` for generic
30	  fallbacks. Never hardcode model names.
31	  - All stage results use `build_stage_outcome()`. Never return ad-hoc dicts.
32	  - All graph nodes use `build_node_data()` / `build_origin()` / `build_provenance()`.
33	  - Direct SQL access is confined to `packages/storage/database.py`. Domain code never touches S
34

## Anti-Pattern Scan Results

### 1. `os.environ` deep in domain code (Anti-pattern #7)

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/228/core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`

- **Line 211:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL")`
  Used inside `extract_causal_relationships()` to conditionally set `cache_control` on a message dict.

- **Line 218:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL")`
  Used inside `extract_causal_relationships()` to set `api_base`.

- **Line 220:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_API_KEY")`
  Used inside `extract_causal_relationships()` to set `api_key`.

- **Line 387:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL")`
  Used inside `DriverIndicatorExtractionTool._build_full_message_list()` to conditionally set `cache_control`.

These are domain/core-layer functions reading environment variables directly rather than receiving configuration from the edges. This violates anti-pattern #7.

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/228/packages/inference/llm/litellm_preflight.py`

- **Line 120:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL", "")`
- **Line 139:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_API_KEY", "")`
- **Line 140:** `os.environ.get("LITELLM_MASTER_KEY", "")`

These are in `run_litellm_preflight()` which is an infrastructure/service-layer function. The function already accepts `proxy_url`, `api_key`, and `master_key` as parameters and falls back to env vars, so this is borderline -- the env vars serve as defaults for optional parameters. This is more acceptable than the `indicator_extraction.py` cases since this module is explicitly an infrastructure preflight check, not domain code. **Marginal -- flag for awareness but lower severity.**

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/228/tests/test_live_graph_entity_process.py`

- **Lines 25-26, 204-209:** Multiple `os.environ.get(...)` calls.

These are in a test file's gating/setup logic. **Not a violation** -- tests are "edge" code.

### 2. Global mutation + lazy imports (Anti-pattern #4)

- **Lines 40-82:** Module-level `None` sentinels (`run_agent_single_scope_retrieval`, `LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent`, `EvidenceDownloadConfig`, `LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig`, `LiteratureQueryConfig`) mutated via `global` inside `_get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval()` (line 48) and `_get_retrieval_components()` (line 57-58). Both functions use `global _x` + inline imports.

The instructions say this file is already tracked as a known issue: "Known issue in driver_indicator_literature.py -- skip that, already tracked." **Skipping per instructions.**

---

### 3. Defensive string wrapping on comparisons (Anti-pattern #3)

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/228/packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py`

- **Line 46:** `str(node.type or "").strip().lower()` in `_node_type()` -- `node.type` comes from a `NodeRecord` which is a typed dataclass field. If this field is already a `str`, wrapping it is unnecessary.

- **Line 67:** `str(edge.type or "").strip().upper()` in `_edge_type()` -- same rationale; `EdgeRecord.type` is already typed.

- **Line 75:** `str(node.type or "").strip().lower() != "driver"` in `_is_question_definition_driver()` -- duplicates the wrapping already done in `_node_type()`.

- **Line 119:** `str(node.type or "").strip().lower()` -- third repetition of the same pattern on `node.type`.

However, these are **borderline**: `NodeRecord` and `EdgeRecord` are storage records that may be constructed from database rows or JSON deserialization where `None` or non-string types could appear. The `.type` field is declared as `str` but could be `None` at runtime from nullable DB columns. Given that these are records from the storage layer being validated, this defensive wrapping on potentially untyped/nullable storage data is arguably justified. **Not flagging as a violation** since these are untrusted/semi-trusted storage records being validated.

**File:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/228/packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py`

- **Line 82:** `str(graph_kind or "generic").strip().lower()` in `_normalize_graph_kind()` -- `graph_kind` is a `str` parameter. The `or "generic"` part provides a default, but wrapping a known `str` parameter in `str()` is unnecessary. **Mild violation**, but the function is explicitly a normalizer.

These write per-driver result files as JSON to the filesystem. However, these files are written to `question_dir / "driver_indicators" / ...` which is a run working directory, so this may be acceptable as ephemeral run artifacts rather than "persistent state." **Borderline -- flag for awareness.** If these files are relied upon by downstream stages (rather than being debug artifacts), they should go through the database.

109	def run_litellm_preflight(
110	    *,
111	    model_set: str | None = None,
112	    probe_tier: str | LLMTier = LLMTier.MEDIUM,
113	    probe_task_ids: Sequence[str] | None = None,
114	    proxy_url: str | None = None,
115	    api_key: str | None = None,
116	    master_key: str | None = None,
117	    timeout_seconds: float = 15.0,
118	) -> dict[str, Any]:
119	    """Validate LiteLLM readiness, model aliases, and tiny provider completions."""
120	    resolved_proxy = str(proxy_url or os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL", "") or "").strip().rstrip("/")
121	    if not resolved_proxy:
122	        raise LiteLLMPreflightError("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL is required for live provider preflight")

{
  "title": "Code quality sweep: remove dead code, defensive wrapping, and env var sniffing across codebase",
  "team": "Spire_Elijah",
  "assignee": "sumeet@sumeetsaini.com",
  "project": "Elijah Pipeline Development",
  "priority": "3",
  "description": "## What this is\n\nSystematic sweep of the codebase for anti-patterns documented in `lookout.md`. The SPI-228 port review surfaced several instances, but these patterns likely exist elsewhere too.\n\n## Anti-patterns to scan for\n\n1. **Dead code** — functions defined but never called, left behind with \"compatibility\" or \"legacy\" comments. Grep for callers; if none, delete.\n2. **Defensive `str(x or \"\").strip()` wrapping** — on values that are already typed strings. Adds noise, no safety benefit.\n3. **`os.environ` deep in domain code** — env vars read inside domain functions instead of at config/CLI edges.\n4. **Module-level model resolution constants** — `MY_MODEL = resolve_task_model(...)` at import time.\n5. **Inline system prompts** — hardcoded prompt strings instead of loading from `prompts/` via the prompt store.\n6. **Global mutation lazy imports** — `global _x` with `Any | None` sentinels instead of function-scoped imports.\n\n## Known instances (from SPI-228 review)\n\n- `extract_causal_relationships()` in `indicator_extraction.py` — dead, never called (SPI-250)\n- `os.environ` in `_build_full_message_list()` in `indicator_extraction.py` (SPI-250)\n- Circular import lazy globals in `driver_indicator_literature.py` (SPI-249)\n\n## Approach\n\nGrep the full codebase for each pattern, file tickets or fix in place depending on scope. Reference `lookout.md` as the authoritative list."
}
```
